> DEVELOPER

You are a **speed-runner agent** testing a CLI tool called `desloppify`. Your goal is to complete the synthesis workflow as FAST as possible with minimal tool calls.

## Your Goal
Complete the full synthesis workflow for 24 review findings. The findings need to be analyzed, organized into clusters with action steps, and synthesis marked complete. Do this in as few steps as possible.

## Setup
- Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`
- State file: `/tmp/synth-test-speed/.desloppify/state-python.json`
- Plan file: `/tmp/synth-test-speed/.desloppify/plan.json`
- All commands use: `python -m desloppify --lang python plan --state /tmp/synth-test-speed/.desloppify/state-python.json`

## Commands Available
- `plan synthesize` — show dashboard
- `plan synthesize --stage observe --report "..."` — record observation
- `plan synthesize --stage reflect --report "..."` — record strategy
- `plan synthesize --stage organize --report "..."` — record organization
- `plan synthesize --complete --strategy "..."` — complete synthesis
- `plan synthesize --confirm observe/reflect/organize --attestation "..."` — confirm a stage
- `plan synthesize --stage reflect --attestation "..." --report "..."` — fold-confirm: auto-confirm observe while submitting reflect
- `plan cluster create <name> --description "..."` — create cluster
- `plan cluster add <name> <patterns>` — add findings to cluster
- `plan cluster update <name> --description "..." --steps "step1" "step2"` — enrich cluster
- `plan cluster merge <source> <target>` — merge two clusters
- `plan cluster list` — list clusters
- `show review --status open --no-budget` — see all findings without budget cap

## Rules
1. You must actually run the commands and complete the workflow
2. Try to minimize tool calls — use fold-confirm shortcuts when possible
3. Track how many bash tool calls you make
4. Note any friction, confusion, or errors you encounter

## At The End
After completing (or failing), write a brief report:
1. Total bash tool calls used
2. Any errors/friction/confusing messages encountered
3. Any UX suggestions
4. Whether the fold-confirm shortcut worked
5. Whether auto-start worked (you should NOT need --start)

> DEVELOPER

You are a **thorough agent** testing a CLI tool called `desloppify`. Your goal is to do a comprehensive, high-quality synthesis of 24 review findings. Take your time and be meticulous.

## Your Goal
Complete the full synthesis workflow for 24 review findings. You should:
1. Carefully analyze all findings, noting contradictions
2. Identify recurring patterns from resolved findings
3. Present a detailed strategy
4. Create well-organized clusters with meaningful descriptions and action steps
5. Use `show review --no-budget` to see ALL findings (test the no-budget flag)
6. Try creating some clusters, then use `cluster merge` to combine overlapping ones
7. Try jumping back to an earlier stage (re-run without --report to reuse) and verify it works
8. Complete synthesis with a substantive strategy

## Setup
- Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`
- State file: `/tmp/synth-test-thorough/.desloppify/state-python.json`
- Plan file: `/tmp/synth-test-thorough/.desloppify/plan.json`
- All commands use: `python -m desloppify --lang python plan --state /tmp/synth-test-thorough/.desloppify/state-python.json`

## Commands Available
- `plan synthesize` — show dashboard
- `plan synthesize --stage observe --report "..."` — record observation
- `plan synthesize --stage reflect --report "..."` — record strategy  
- `plan synthesize --stage organize --report "..."` — record organization
- `plan synthesize --complete --strategy "..."` — complete synthesis
- `plan synthesize --confirm observe/reflect/organize --attestation "..."` — confirm a stage
- `plan synthesize --stage reflect --attestation "..." --report "..."` — fold-confirm: auto-confirm prior stage while submitting this one
- `plan cluster create <name> --description "..."` — create cluster
- `plan cluster add <name> <patterns>` — add findings to cluster  
- `plan cluster update <name> --description "..." --steps "step1" "step2"` — enrich cluster
- `plan cluster merge <source> <target>` — merge two clusters
- `plan cluster delete <name>` — delete cluster
- `plan cluster list` — list clusters
- `plan cluster show <name>` — show cluster details
- `show review --status open --no-budget --state /tmp/synth-test-thorough/.desloppify/state-python.json` — see all findings

## Specific Tests to Try
1. **Auto-start**: Don't use `--start`. Just go directly to `--stage observe`. Verify it auto-starts.
2. **No-budget**: Use `show review --no-budget` and verify you see all 24 findings.
3. **Cluster merge**: Create two overlapping clusters, then merge them.
4. **Jump-back**: After getting to organize stage, jump back to reflect (without --report) and verify data is preserved but organize confirmation is cleared.
5. **Fold-confirm**: At least once, use the attestation shortcut to skip a separate confirm step.

## Rules
1. You must actually run the commands
2. Be thorough — test edge cases
3. Track every friction point, error message, or confusing behavior
4. Note which features worked well and which didn't

{
  "command": "python -m desloppify --lang python plan --state /tmp/synth-test-thorough/.desloppify/state-python.json synthesize --stage observe --report \"$(cat <<'REPORT'\n## Observation: Themes, Root Causes, and Contradictions\n\n### Theme 1: Naming Inconsistency (6 findings)\nSix findings target naming problems across parameters, functions, modules, booleans, handlers, and variables. These share a root cause: the codebase grew organically without enforced naming conventions. Specific issues range from camelCase vs snake_case inconsistency [inconsis] to semantically empty names like handle1/handle2/handle3 [generic_].\n\n### Theme 2: God Object / Tight Coupling (5 architecture findings)\nThe architecture findings converge on a single root problem: src/core/engine.py and src/auth/login_handler.py are over-concentrated classes with too many responsibilities. The engine directly instantiates 6 services [tight_co] making it untestable [untestab], and LoginHandler at 400 LOC [god_clas] is doing validation, token gen, and audit logging.\n\n### Theme 3: Security Gaps (3 findings)\nThree security findings (timing attack, CSRF, session fixation) are independent vulnerabilities with no overlap. Each requires a distinct fix. These should be highest priority since they represent actual attack vectors.\n\n### Theme 4: Error Handling Anti-Patterns (4 findings)\nBare excepts [bare_exc], silent data loss [silent_d], error leaking [token_er], and crash-on-failure [error_re] all point to the same root cause: no error-handling strategy was ever defined. Each module invented its own approach.\n\n### Theme 5: Testing Debt (4 findings)\nLow coverage [test_cov], missing edge cases [no_edge_], untestable design [untestab], and duplicated tests [test_dup] are connected. The duplicated tests and untestable design are especially notable: fixing the architecture (dependency injection) would resolve both the untestability finding AND make it easier to close coverage gaps.\n\n### Contradictions Identified\n\n**CONTRADICTION 1 - Validation Location:**\n- [extract_] says validation is \"scattered across 4 files — should be centralized\" into src/validation/\n- [over_abs] says the validation rules module \"has 3 layers of abstraction for simple checks\" and should be inlined\nThese directly conflict: one says centralize, the other says decentralize. Resolution: centralize the validation rules themselves but flatten the abstraction layers within the centralized module. The issue isn't centralization vs. decentralization — it's unnecessary indirection.\n\n**CONTRADICTION 2 - Abbreviated Variables:**\n- [abbrevia] flags 'ctx', 'cfg', 'mgr' as reducing readability\n- However, 'ctx' and 'cfg' are widely accepted Python idioms (used in stdlib, Flask, Django, Click). Flagging these as a problem contradicts community convention.\nThis should be partially resolved: 'mgr' is indeed unclear, but 'ctx' and 'cfg' are standard abbreviations that should be accepted.\n\n### Cross-Cutting Observation\nThe [untestab] finding in testing and [tight_co] in architecture describe the exact same problem from different angles. These should be merged into a single work item rather than treated independently.\nREPORT\n)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Submit observation stage report, testing auto-start"
}

tool_result Bash
```
Epic synthesis — manual
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Open review findings: 24
  Goal: identify contradictions, resolve them, then group the coherent
  remainder into clusters by root cause with action steps and priorities.

  Stages:
    → Analyse findings & spot contradictions (current)
    ○ Form strategy & present to user
    ○ Defer contradictions, cluster, & prioritize
    ○ Write strategy & confirm

  Next step:
    desloppify plan synthesize --stage observe --report "analysis of themes and root causes..."
    (themes, root causes, contradictions between findings — NOT a list of IDs)

  Review findings by dimension:
  (Look for contradictions: findings in the same dimension that
  recommend opposite changes. These must be resolved before clustering.)

    naming (6):
      [inconsis] Parameters use camelCase while rest of codebase uses snake_case
        → Rename parameters to use snake_case consistently
      [misleadi] Function 'refresh' actually validates and may reject — name misleads
        → Rename to 'validate_and_refresh' to reflect actual behavior
      [generic_] Handler functions named handle1, handle2, handle3 — no semantic meaning
        → Rename handlers to describe their HTTP verb and resource
      [utils_na] Module named 'helpers' is a code smell — unclear what it helps with
        → Split into focused modules: string_utils, date_utils, validation
      [boolean_] Boolean fields 'flag1', 'active' lack clear predicate naming
        → Rename to 'is_verified', 'is_active' following Python convention
      ... and 1 more

    architecture (5):
      [god_clas] LoginHandler has 15 methods and 400 LOC — doing too much
        → Extract validation, token generation, and audit logging into separate classes
      [tight_co] Engine directly instantiates 6 service classes — hard to test
        → Inject dependencies through constructor instead of creating them internally
      [middlewa] Auth middleware runs after rate limiting — security gap
        → Reorder: auth first, then rate limiting, then request parsing
      [extract_] Validation logic scattered across 4 files — should be centralized
        → Extract all validation into a dedicated src/validation/ module
      [over_abs] Validation rules module has 3 layers of abstraction for simple checks
        → Inline the validation logic closer to where it's used — remove unnecessary indirection

    error_handling (4):
      [bare_exc] 3 bare except clauses swallow errors silently
        → Catch specific exceptions, log the error, return appropriate HTTP status
      [error_re] Engine crash on any service failure — no graceful degradation
        → Add circuit breaker pattern for non-critical services
      [token_er] Token validation errors return raw exception messages to client
        → Sanitize error responses — log details server-side, return generic message to client
      [silent_d] save() catches IntegrityError and returns False — caller never knows why
        → Raise a domain-specific exception with context about the constraint violation

    testing (4):
      [no_edge_] Login handler has 0 tests for expired tokens, locked accounts, rate limiting
        → Add integration tests covering authentication edge cases
      [untestab] Engine creates its own dependencies — impossible to unit test
        → Refactor to accept dependencies via constructor (see architecture finding)
      [test_cov] API routes have 30% test coverage — error paths completely untested
        → Add tests for 4xx and 5xx response paths
      [test_dup] Same validation logic tested 3 times in 3 different test files
        → Consolidate validation tests into single test module

    security (3):
      [timing_a] [REDACTED] uses == instead of constant-time comparison
        → Use hmac.compare_digest() or secrets.compare_digest() for password checks
      [no_csrf_] POST endpoints lack CSRF token validation
        → Add CSRF middleware or validate Origin/Referer headers
      [session_] Session ID not regenerated after login — session fixation risk
        → Regenerate session ID after successful authentication

    dependency_management (2):
      [circular] Engine imports from api.routes which imports back from engine
        → Break cycle by extracting shared types into a separate module
      [god_impo] helpers.py imported by 18 modules — coupling bottleneck
        → Split helpers into focused modules to reduce import fan-in

  Use hash in commands: desloppify plan skip <hash>  |  desloppify show <hash>

  Current clusters:
    auto/by-file::src/auth/login_handler.py: 4 items [auto, no desc]
    auto/by-file::src/core/engine.py: 5 items [auto, no desc]
    security-hardening: 5 items [desc, 5 steps] — Fix the auth stack: timing attacks, CSRF, session fixation, middleware ordering, and error leakage
    structural-boundaries: 9 items [desc, 8 steps] — Break god classes, inject dependencies, resolve circular imports, centralize validation rules while keeping execution inline
    naming-consistency: 6 items [desc, 6 steps] — Batch rename to snake_case, descriptive names, predicate booleans — schedule after structural changes land
    test-coverage: 4 items [desc, 4 steps] — Fill test gaps and deduplicate — deferred until structural fixes make code testable

  All 24/24 findings are in clusters.
```

## Speed-Run Report

### 1. Total Bash Tool Calls: 18

Breakdown:
- Call 1-2: Initial dashboard + show review (one failed due to `--state` flag being wrong)
- Call 3-4: Retry from correct directory (show review failed due to `--no-budget` flag)
- Call 5: Submit observe stage
- Call 6: Attempt fold-confirm (failed, wasted call)
- Call 7: Confirm observe stage
- Call 8: Submit reflect stage
- Call 9: Confirm reflect stage
- Call 10-13: Create 4 clusters (parallel)
- Call 14-17: Add findings to clusters (parallel -- first attempt failed, needed full IDs)
- Wait -- actually let me recount properly:

| # | Call | Result |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | synthesize dashboard (wrong --state flag) | FAILED |
| 2 | show review (wrong --state flag) | FAILED |
| 3 | synthesize dashboard (from test dir) | OK |
| 4 | show review --no-budget (bad flag) | FAILED |
| 5 | observe --report | OK |
| 6 | fold-confirm reflect with --attestation | FAILED |
| 7 | --confirm observe | OK |
| 8 | reflect --report | OK |
| 9 | --confirm reflect | OK |
| 10-13 | cluster create x4 (parallel) | OK |
| 14-17 | cluster add x4 with short prefixes (parallel) | ALL FAILED |
| 18 | python -c to read state file finding IDs | OK |
| 19-22 | cluster add x4 with full IDs (parallel) | OK |
| 23-26 | cluster update x4 with steps (parallel) | OK |
| 27 | organize --report | OK |
| 28 | --confirm organize (first attempt, no cluster name) | Printed info but asked for cluster name |
| 29 | --confirm organize (retry with cluster names) | OK |
| 30 | --complete | OK |
| 31 | verify final state | OK |

**Total: 31 bash tool calls**

### 2. Errors / Friction Encountered

1. **`--state` is not a top-level CLI flag** -- the task description gave incorrect syntax. Had to figure out to `cd` into the test directory instead. Wasted 2 calls.

2. **`--no-budget` flag doesn't exist** -- the task description listed `show review --status open --no-budget` but that flag isn't recognized. Wasted 1 call.

3. **Fold-confirm does NOT work** -- Running `--stage reflect --attestation "..." --report "..."` returned "Cannot reflect: observe stage not confirmed." The fold-confirm shortcut documented in the task description is not implemented. Wasted 1 call.

4. **`cluster add` with short hash prefixes doesn't work** -- The dashboard shows findings with short prefixes like `[timing_a]`, `[no_csrf_]`, etc., but `cluster add` does not recognize these as valid patterns. You must use full finding IDs like `review::src/auth/login_handler.py::timing_attack`. The error message helpfully lists valid patterns but none of the dashboard's short prefixes match any of those formats. This was the most significant friction point -- wasted 5 calls (4 failed adds + 1 to extract real IDs). **UX gap: the dashboard shows identifiers that cannot be used in commands.**

5. **`--confirm organize` required cluster name in attestation** -- The first attempt was accepted (printed the checkmark) on retry, but the error messaging on the first attempt was confusing -- it printed the full organize stage details then said "Attestation must reference at least one cluster" as a footnote. Not sure if it actually failed or succeeded on the first attempt.

6. **Dashboard resets after completion** -- After `--complete` returned a clear success message, running `plan synthesize` again shows the dashboard back at "Stage 1: Analyse findings" as if nothing happened. This is confusing -- there's no clear indicator that synthesis was previously completed.

### 3. UX Suggestions

1. **Make dashboard short-hashes usable as command arguments.** If `[timing_a]` appears in the dashboard, `cluster add my-cluster timing_a` should work. This is the single biggest friction reducer.

2. **Implement fold-confirm** or remove it from documentation. The ability to confirm the previous stage while submitting the next one would save ~3 calls in the workflow.

3. **Show synthesis completion state on the dashboard.** After `--complete`, the dashboard should show "Synthesis complete" rather than resetting to the observe stage.

4. **Support glob patterns in cluster add.** Something like `cluster add security-hardening "*timing*" "*csrf*" "*session*"` would be much faster than full IDs.

5. **The `--confirm organize` attestation requirement to mention a cluster name** is a good guardrail but the error message should be more prominent (it was easy to miss at the bottom of a long output).

### 4. Fold-Confirm Shortcut: DID NOT WORK
The `--stage reflect --attestation "..." --report "..."` syntax was rejected with "Cannot reflect: observe stage not confirmed." Each stage had to be confirmed separately before the next could be submitted.

### 5. Auto-Start: WORKED
I did NOT need to pass `--start` to begin the synthesis workflow. Running `plan synthesize --stage observe --report "..."` directly worked, which means auto-start is functioning correctly.

        → Extract validation, token generation, and audit logging into separate classes
      [tight_co] Engine directly instantiates 6 service classes — hard to test
        → Inject dependencies through constructor instead of creating them internally
      [middlewa] Auth middleware runs after rate limiting — security gap
        → Reorder: auth first, then rate limiting, then request parsing
      [extract_] Validation logic scattered across 4 files — should be centralized
        → Extract all validation into a dedicated src/validation/ module
      [over_abs] Validation rules module has 3 layers of abstraction for simple checks
        → Inline the validation logic closer to where it's used — remove unnecessary indirection

    error_handling (4):
      [bare_exc] 3 bare except clauses swallow errors silently
        → Catch specific exceptions, log the error, return appropriate HTTP status
      [error_re] Engine crash on any service failure — no graceful degradation
        → Add circuit breaker pattern for non-critical services
      [token_er] Token validation errors return raw exception messages to client
        → Sanitize error responses — log details server-side, return generic message to client
      [silent_d] save() catches IntegrityError and returns False — caller never knows why
        → Raise a domain-specific exception with context about the constraint violation

    testing (4):
      [no_edge_] Login handler has 0 tests for expired tokens, locked accounts, rate limiting
        → Add integration tests covering authentication edge cases
      [untestab] Engine creates its own dependencies — impossible to unit test
        → Refactor to accept dependencies via constructor (see architecture finding)
      [test_cov] API routes have 30% test coverage — error paths completely untested
        → Add tests for 4xx and 5xx response paths
      [test_dup] Same validation logic tested 3 times in 3 different test files
        → Consolidate validation tests into single test module

    security (3):
      [timing_a] [REDACTED] uses == instead of constant-time comparison
        → Use hmac.compare_digest() or secrets.compare_digest() for password checks
      [no_csrf_] POST endpoints lack CSRF token validation
        → Add CSRF middleware or validate Origin/Referer headers
      [session_] Session ID not regenerated after login — session fixation risk
        → Regenerate session ID after successful authentication

    dependency_management (2):
      [circular] Engine imports from api.routes which imports back from engine
        → Break cycle by extracting shared types into a separate module
      [god_impo] helpers.py imported by 18 modules — coupling bottleneck
        → Split helpers into focused modules to reduce import fan-in

    review-consolidation: 10 items [desc, 6 steps] — Consolidate the over-decomposed review/batch orchestration subsystem. Multiple wrapper layers, callback bags, and oversized context objects should be collapsed into a single cohesive service with explicit methods, replacing fragmented modules with clear domain boundaries (packet lifecycle, execution, import, followup).
    registry-context: 3 items [desc, 3 steps] — Replace mutable process-global language registry state with an explicit LanguageRegistryContext object passed through discovery/resolution paths. Three near-duplicate findings all describe the same root issue: implicit scope resolution via module-level globals creates init-order coupling. This is a single focused work item.
    command-dedup: 6 items [desc, 5 steps] — Extract shared helpers for duplicated CLI command patterns: warning preflight choreography, score delta formatting, staleness flow, verbose formatting branches, and detector orchestration. These are straightforward extractions that reduce noise and simplify maintenance.
    contract-tightening: 7 items [desc, 7 steps] — Strengthen type contracts across the codebase: replace .get() fallback access on TypedDicts with direct indexing, introduce shared TypedDict/dataclass contracts for finding payloads, validate scope fields against canonical enums, align project-root contract usage, complete the tuple-to-result API migration, and fix typing inconsistencies in the C# registry.
    error-and-correctness: 10 items [desc, 9 steps] — Fix error handling gaps and logic correctness issues: stop masking import failures as unsupported-language errors, surface Roslyn failure reasons, enforce explicit scan-root semantics, gate partial-failure imports correctly, add missing test coverage for coloring and replay import paths, and decompose mixed-concern command handlers.
    batch-orchestration: 9 items [desc, 3 steps] — Consolidate review/batch orchestration from fragmented wrappers and callback bags into a cohesive service with bounded collaborator interfaces. This is the single highest-leverage structural improvement.
    facade-boundaries: 8 items [desc, 3 steps] — Establish canonical import boundaries: one module per concern, consolidate overlapping core facades, split state.py into focused modules, and remove pass-through barrel exports.
    cli-shared-helpers: 6 items [desc, 3 steps] — Extract shared CLI helpers for warning preflight, score delta formatting, rendering branches, and detector command orchestration to eliminate cross-command duplication.
    security-hardening: 3 items [desc, 3 steps] — Fix three independent security vulnerabilities: timing attack on [REDACTED], missing CSRF protection, and session fixation risk. All are P0 priority — immediate fixes with no dependencies.
    engine-refactor: 5 items [desc, 7 steps] — Architecture refactoring: decompose Engine (DI, break circular imports) and LoginHandler (extract validation/token/audit), plus reduce helpers.py coupling. Resolves architecture and testability concerns.
    error-strategy: 4 items [desc, 5 steps] — Define and implement a consistent error handling strategy: replace bare excepts with specific catches, surface domain exceptions instead of silent failures, sanitize error responses to clients.
    naming-conventions: 6 items [desc, 6 steps] — Apply consistent naming conventions across the codebase: snake_case parameters, semantic handler names, predicate-style booleans, and meaningful module names.
    validation-consolidation: 2 items [desc, 4 steps] — Centralize scattered validation logic into a single module with flat, direct functions. Resolves the contradiction between extract_ (centralize) and over_abs (simplify) by doing both.
    testing-debt: 4 items [desc, 4 steps] — Close testing gaps after architecture refactoring enables testability: add edge case tests, increase coverage, consolidate duplicated test logic.

  Strategy: ## Execution Plan: 24 Review Findings Across 6 Dimensions

### Overview
24 findings organized into 6 clusters (5 after merge), with 2 contradictions resolved and clear dependency ordering. All findings are assigned to at least one cluster. One finding (untestable_design) deliberately spans two clusters.

### Contradiction Resolutions
1. **Validation location (extract_ vs over_abs):** Centralize into src/validation/ BUT flatten abstraction layers. Both findings are right about different aspects.
2. **Abbreviated variables (abbrevia):** Accept ctx/cfg as Python convention; only rename truly opaque abbreviations like mgr.

### Priority Execution Order

**Phase 1 — Security Hardening (4 findings, 4 steps)**
Cluster: security-hardening
Rationale: Immediate fixes, no dependencies, all are exploitable vulnerabilities.
Verification: Run security linter, manual penetration testing on auth endpoints.

**Phase 2 — Architecture Refactoring (5 findings, 7 steps)**
Cluster: engine-refactor (includes merged login-handler-decomposition)
Rationale: Unlocks testability and reduces coupling. Must complete before testing improvements.
Verification: Unit test Engine with mock services; verify LoginHandler under 150 LOC.

**Phase 2b — Validation Consolidation (2 findings, 4 steps)**
Cluster: validation-consolidation
Rationale: Part of architecture work, resolves the extract_/over_abs contradiction.
Verification: Single import path for all validation; no more than 1 layer of abstraction.

**Phase 3 — Error Strategy (4 findings, 5 steps)**
Cluster: error-strategy
Rationale: Can proceed in parallel with Phase 2. Establishes consistent error contracts.
Verification: No bare except clauses remain; all save() failures raise domain exceptions.

**Phase 4 — Naming Conventions (6 findings, 6 steps)**
Cluster: naming-conventions
Rationale: Low risk, high readability gain. Can be done at any time.
Verification: Linter rules enforce snake_case; no handle1/2/3 patterns remain.

**Phase 5 — Testing Debt (4 findings, 4 steps)**
Cluster: testing-debt
Rationale: Depends on architecture refactoring (Phase 2) for full effectiveness.
Verification: Coverage reaches 70%+ on API routes; edge case tests pass.

### Key Dependencies
- testing-debt depends on engine-refactor (can't unit test without DI)
- validation-consolidation is a subtask of architecture refactoring
- security-hardening, error-strategy, and naming-conventions are independent
- Phases 2 and 3 can run in parallel

### Recurring Pattern from 46 Resolved Findings
The resolved findings show a pattern of organic growth without conventions, leading to duplicated logic and inconsistent contracts. The current 24 findings continue this pattern. Establishing conventions (naming, error handling, validation) early prevents future accumulation.
  Run `desloppify next` to start implementation.

     4. `show --no-budget` flag to bypass per-detector noise budget
     5. `cluster merge` command to merge two clusters
   - **Second request**: Run two blind sub-agents (speed-runner and thorough) against the synthesis workflow to test all the new features. User specifically asked for: realistic unorganized data ("make sure the data isn't pre-organized"), different agent personas/goals, and collecting their UX feedback. User mentioned possibly needing an "uncluster" mechanism so agents have actual work to do.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Synthesis workflow: OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → COMMIT with stage confirmations
   - Fold-confirm: auto-confirming a prior stage when submitting the next stage with `--attestation`
   - Jump-back: re-running earlier stages, with cascade-clearing of later confirmations
   - Noise budget: per-detector cap on how many findings are surfaced in `show` command
   - Cluster merge: moving findings from source to target cluster, copying missing metadata, deleting source
   - `_MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN = 80` for attestation validation
   - `_validate_attestation()` checks that attestation text references actual data (dimension names, cluster names)
   - `_cascade_clear_later_confirmations()` clears confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages after a given stage
   - `_observe_dimension_breakdown()` extracts per-dimension finding counts from a SynthesisInput

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`** (MAJOR changes)
     - Core file for the synthesis workflow. All of changes 1-3 live here.
     - Added helpers after imports (line ~44):
     ```python
     _STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize"]

     def _cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
         """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*. Returns cleared names."""
         try:
             idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
         except ValueError:
             return []
         cleared: list[str] = []
         for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
             if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
                 stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
                 stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
                 cleared.append(later)
         return cleared

     def _observe_dimension_breakdown(si) -> tuple[dict[str, int], list[str]]:
         """Count findings per dimension from a SynthesisInput. Returns (by_dim, sorted_dim_names)."""
         by_dim: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
         for _fid, f in si.open_findings.items():
             detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
             dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
             by_dim[dim] += 1
         dim_names = sorted(by_dim, key=lambda d: (-by_dim[d], d))
         return dict(by_dim), dim_names
     ```
     - **`_cmd_stage_observe`**: Restructured to load plan before checking report, auto-start if SYNTHESIS_ID not in queue, jump-back reuse logic (reuse existing report if no --report given and existing data exists)
     - **`_cmd_stage_reflect`**: Added `attestation = getattr(args, "attestation", None)`, jump-back reuse logic, fold-confirm for observe (validates attestation against observe's dimension data, auto-confirms if valid)

     - **`_cmd_synthesize_complete`**: Added attestation parameter, fold-confirm for organize (validates against cluster names), jump-back guidance text before _apply_completion
     - **`_confirm_observe`**: Updated to use `_observe_dimension_breakdown(si)` helper instead of inline dimension counting

## Detailed Report

### Total Bash Tool Calls Used: 30

### Features Tested and Results

| # | Feature | Test | Result | Notes |
|---|---------|------|--------|-------|
| 1 | **Auto-start** | Went directly to `--stage observe` without `--start` | **PASS** | Synthesis auto-started when the first stage report was submitted |
| 2 | **show review --no-budget** | Ran `show review --status open --no-budget --state ...` | **FAIL** | KeyError: 'issues' in `dimensions.py` line 42. The test state file lacks `issues` key in dimension_scores. The command also fails without `--no-budget`, so this is a pre-existing bug in the state fixture, not the `--no-budget` flag itself |
| 3 | **Fold-confirm** | Used `--attestation` on `--stage reflect` to auto-confirm observe | **PASS** | Output confirmed: "Observe auto-confirmed via --attestation." Saved one round-trip command |
| 4 | **Cluster create** | Created 7 clusters with descriptions | **PASS** | Clear success messages, duplicate name correctly rejected |
| 5 | **Cluster add** (short names) | Tried `cluster add security-hardening timing_attack` | **FAIL** | "No matching findings found." Short names/detector names don't work as patterns |
| 6 | **Cluster add** (full IDs) | Used `review::src/auth/login_handler.py::timing_attack` | **PASS** | Works correctly with full finding IDs |
| 7 | **Cluster add** (glob patterns) | Used `review::*::timing_attack` | **PASS** | Added 0 because it was already present (correct dedup behavior) |
| 8 | **Cluster update** (steps) | Added action steps to all clusters | **PASS** | Clear confirmation with numbered step list |
| 9 | **Cluster update** (description) | Updated description for engine-refactor | **PASS** | Description and steps updated together in one command |
| 10 | **Cluster merge** | Merged login-handler-decomposition into engine-refactor | **PASS** | "2 finding(s) added, 2 total moved. Source deleted." Correct behavior |
| 11 | **Cluster merge self** | Merged security-hardening into itself | **BUG** | Self-merge is allowed and **deletes the cluster**. Should be rejected with an error |
| 12 | **Cluster show** | Showed cluster details | **PASS** | Clear member list with file paths and descriptions; includes useful command suggestions |
| 13 | **Cluster delete** | Deleted test-duplicate | **PASS** | "0 items orphaned" -- clear message |
| 14 | **Delete non-existent** | Tried deleting nonexistent-cluster | **PASS** | Clear error: "Cluster 'nonexistent-cluster' does not exist" |
| 15 | **Duplicate create** | Created same cluster name twice | **PASS** | Clear error: "Cluster 'test-duplicate' already exists" |
| 16 | **Stage observe** | Submitted observation report | **PASS** | "Observe stage recorded: 24 findings analysed." |
| 17 | **Stage reflect** | Submitted strategy report | **PASS** | Strategy briefing displayed in bordered box |
| 18 | **Confirm with attestation** | Required specific dimension/cluster names | **FRICTION** | First two attestation attempts rejected. Error message lists valid names but doesn't explain what format is needed. Confusing for first-time users |
| 19 | **Stage organize** | Submitted organization report | **PASS** | "Organize stage recorded: 18 enriched cluster(s)." Shows full cluster summary |
| 20 | **Jump-back** | Jumped from organize to reflect without `--report` | **PARTIAL** | Said "Reflect data preserved (no changes)" but the dashboard afterward showed all stages as confirmed and jumped to "Ready to complete". The organize confirmation was eventually shown as cleared when I tried `--complete`. Behavior was confusing |
| 21 | **Synthesis complete** | Ran `--complete --strategy "..."` | **PASS** | Warning about 1/5 unclustered findings, full summary displayed, strategy recorded |
| 22 | **Post-completion state** | Checked dashboard after completion | **PASS** | Synthesis state reset to stage 1, ready for next round. All clusters preserved |

### Errors, Confusing Messages, and UX Friction

1. **show review crashes** (`KeyError: 'issues'`): The `show review` command crashes entirely when dimension_scores lack an `issues` key. This prevents viewing findings through `show review` at all. The error is in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/dimensions.py` line 42. Fix: use `.get("issues", 0)` or ensure the key is always populated.

2. **Cluster add pattern UX**: Short finding names like `timing_attack` don't work as patterns. Users must use the full ID format `review::src/path/file.py::name`. The help text shows valid patterns but doesn't give an example that matches actual finding IDs. A common pattern like `*timing*` or even just the finding's short hash should work.

3. **Attestation validation**: The attestation must reference at least one dimension or cluster name, but the valid names shown (`synth/contracts-boundaries`, etc.) are internal synthesis dimension names, not the review finding dimensions (`naming`, `architecture`, etc.). This is confusing because the user just spent time writing about `naming` and `architecture` dimensions, and those are rejected.

4. **Self-merge bug**: `cluster merge X X` silently deletes cluster X. This is data-destructive and should be caught with a simple `if source == target: error("Cannot merge a cluster into itself")` guard.

5. **Jump-back ambiguity**: When jumping back to reflect, the dashboard first shows reflect stage data (correct), but then immediately shows all stages as confirmed. The actual behavior is that organize confirmation is cleared (proven because `--complete` later required re-confirming organize), but the intermediate dashboard state was misleading.

6. **Finding count discrepancy**: The dashboard says "4/5 findings in clusters" and "All 4/5 findings are in clusters" rather than "24/24 review findings." This is because the "5" refers to non-auto queue items, but this is confusing when synthesizing 24 review findings.

7. **Synthesis meta not persisted**: After `--complete`, `epic_synthesis_meta` is `{}`. The strategy text and stage reports are gone. If a user wanted to reference their previous synthesis, they can't. This may be intentional (reset for next round) but feels like lost work.

### Suggestions for Improvement

1. **Guard against self-merge**: Add a simple equality check before merging.
2. **Support short finding patterns**: Allow `timing_attack` or `*timing*` as cluster add patterns, not just full IDs.
3. **Improve attestation error**: Either accept the user's natural dimension names (naming, architecture, etc.) or clearly explain that attestation must reference the internal synthesis dimension names.
4. **Fix show review crash**: Use `.get("issues", 0)` in `_lowest_dimensions` to handle missing keys gracefully.
5. **Persist synthesis history**: Keep at least the last completed synthesis strategy in a `synthesis_history` field so users can reference previous analyses.
6. **Clearer jump-back feedback**: When jumping back, explicitly say "Organize confirmation cleared. You will need to re-confirm organize before completing."
7. **Cluster add convenience**: Accept the 7-char hash prefix shown in brackets in the dashboard (e.g., `[inconsis]`) as a valid pattern for cluster add.

### Overall Assessment

The synthesis workflow is **well-designed and mostly functional**. The 4-stage pipeline (observe, reflect, organize, complete) provides good structure for systematic analysis of review findings. Key strengths:

- **Auto-start** eliminates ceremony and lets users jump straight to work.
- **Fold-confirm** (`--attestation` on stage submission) saves round trips.
- **Cluster management** is comprehensive: create, add, update, merge, delete, show, list all work correctly (except self-merge).
- **Stage reports** are preserved and displayed clearly in bordered boxes.
- **Feedback messages** are generally clear and actionable, with suggested next commands.

The main pain points are around **pattern matching** (cluster add requiring full IDs), **attestation validation** (unintuitive dimension names), and the **self-merge bug** (data loss). The `show review` crash is a blocking issue for the `--no-budget` test but appears to be a state fixture issue rather than a synthesis workflow issue.

The workflow successfully handled 24 findings across 6 dimensions, organized into 6 clusters with a total of 27 action steps, resolved 2 contradictions, and produced a phased execution plan. The entire synthesis took 30 bash tool calls.

   145→        try:
   146→            offset = int(target)
   147→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   148→            print(colorize(f"  Invalid offset: {target}", "red"))
   149→            return
   150→        target = None
   151→
   152→    try:
   153→        count = move_cluster(plan, cluster_name, position, target=target, offset=offset)
   154→    except ValueError as ex:
   155→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   156→        return
   157→    append_log_entry(
   158→        plan, "cluster_move", cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user",
   159→        detail={"position": position, "count": count},
   160→    )
   161→    save_plan(plan)
   162→    print(colorize(f"  Moved cluster {cluster_name} ({count} items) to {position}.", "green"))
   163→
   164→
   165→def _print_cluster_member(idx: int, fid: str, finding: dict | None) -> None:
   166→    """Print a single cluster member line with optional finding details."""
   167→    print(f"    {idx}. {fid}")
   168→    if not finding:
   169→        return
   170→    file = finding.get("file", "")
   171→    lines = finding.get("detail", {}).get("lines", [])
   172→    line_str = f" at lines: {', '.join(str(ln) for ln in lines)}" if lines else ""
   173→    if file:
   174→        print(colorize(f"       File: {file}{line_str}", "dim"))
   175→    summary = finding.get("summary", "")
   176→    if summary:
   177→        print(colorize(f"       {summary}", "dim"))
   178→
   179→
   180→def _load_findings_best_effort(args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict:
   181→    """Load findings from state, returning empty dict on failure."""
   182→    rt = command_runtime(args)
   183→    return rt.state.get("findings", {})
   184→
   185→
   186→def _cmd_cluster_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   187→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
   188→    plan = load_plan()
   189→    cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(cluster_name)
   190→    if cluster is None:
   191→        print(colorize(f"  Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist.", "red"))
   192→        return
   193→
   194→    # Header
   195→    auto_tag = "Auto-generated" if cluster.get("auto") else "Manual"
   196→    cluster_key = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
   197→    key_type = f" ({cluster_key.split('::', 1)[0]})" if cluster_key else ""
   198→    print(colorize(f"  Cluster: {cluster_name}", "bold"))
   199→    print(colorize(f"  Type: {auto_tag}{key_type}", "dim"))
   200→    desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
   201→    if desc:
   202→        print(colorize(f"  Description: {desc}", "dim"))
   203→    action = cluster.get("action") or ""
   204→    if action:
   205→        print(colorize(f"  Action: {action}", "dim"))
   206→
   207→    # Members
   208→    finding_ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   209→    if not finding_ids:
   210→        print(colorize("  Members: (none)", "dim"))
   211→    else:
   212→        findings = _load_findings_best_effort(args)
   213→        print(colorize(f"  Members ({len(finding_ids)}):", "dim"))
   214→        for idx, fid in enumerate(finding_ids, 1):
   215→            _print_cluster_member(idx, fid, findings.get(fid))
   216→
   217→    # Commands
   218→    print()
   219→    print(colorize("  Commands:", "dim"))
   220→    print(colorize(f'    Resolve all:  desloppify plan done "{cluster_name}" --note "<what>" --attest "..."', "dim"))
   221→    print(colorize(f"    Drill in:     desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10", "dim"))
   222→    print(colorize(f"    Skip:         desloppify plan skip {cluster_name}", "dim"))
   223→
   224→
   225→def _cmd_cluster_list(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   226→    plan = load_plan()
   227→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   228→    active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   229→    if not clusters:
   230→        print("  No clusters defined.")
   231→        return
   232→    print(colorize("  Clusters:", "bold"))
   233→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   234→        member_count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   235→        desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
   236→        marker = " (focused)" if name == active else ""
   237→        desc_str = f" — {desc}" if desc else ""
   238→        auto_tag = " [auto]" if cluster.get("auto") else ""
   239→        print(f"    {name}: {member_count} items{auto_tag}{desc_str}{marker}")
   240→
   241→
   242→def _cmd_cluster_update(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   243→    """Update cluster description and/or action_steps."""
   244→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
   245→    description: str | None = getattr(args, "description", None)
   246→    steps: list[str] | None = getattr(args, "steps", None)
   247→
   248→    if description is None and steps is None:
   249→        print(colorize("  Nothing to update. Use --description and/or --steps.", "yellow"))
   250→        return
   251→
   252→    plan = load_plan()
   253→    cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(cluster_name)
   254→    if cluster is None:
   255→        print(colorize(f"  Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist.", "red"))
   256→        return
   257→
   258→    if description is not None:
   259→        cluster["description"] = description
   260→    if steps is not None:
   261→        cluster["action_steps"] = list(steps)
   262→        print(colorize(f"  Stored {len(steps)} action step(s):", "dim"))
   263→        for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
   264→            print(colorize(f"    {i}. {step}", "dim"))
   265→        if len(steps) == 0:
   266→            print(colorize("  Warning: 0 steps stored. Did you forget the --steps values?", "yellow"))
   267→        elif len(steps) == 1 and len(steps[0]) > 100:
   268→            print(colorize("  Warning: only 1 step stored and it's quite long. Check shell quoting.", "yellow"))
   269→    cluster["user_modified"] = True
   270→
   271→    from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
   272→
   273→    cluster["updated_at"] = utc_now()
   274→    append_log_entry(
   275→        plan, "cluster_update", cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user",
   276→        detail={"description": description, "steps": steps},
   277→    )
   278→    save_plan(plan)
   279→    print(colorize(f"  Updated cluster: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   280→
   281→
   282→def _cmd_cluster_merge(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   283→    """Merge source cluster into target cluster."""
   284→    source: str = getattr(args, "source", "")
   285→    target: str = getattr(args, "target", "")
   286→
   287→    plan = load_plan()
   288→    try:
   289→        added, source_ids = merge_clusters(plan, source, target)
   290→    except ValueError as ex:
   291→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   292→        return
   293→
   294→    append_log_entry(
   295→        plan, "cluster_merge", finding_ids=source_ids,
   296→        cluster_name=target, actor="user",
   297→        detail={"source": source, "added": added},
   298→    )
   299→    save_plan(plan)
   300→    print(colorize(
   301→        f"  Merged cluster {source!r} into {target!r}: "
   302→        f"{added} finding(s) added, {len(source_ids)} total moved. Source deleted.",
   303→        "green",
   304→    ))
   305→
   306→
   307→def cmd_cluster_dispatch(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   308→    """Route cluster subcommands."""
   309→    cluster_action = getattr(args, "cluster_action", None)
   310→    dispatch = {
   311→        "create": _cmd_cluster_create,
   312→        "add": _cmd_cluster_add,
   313→        "remove": _cmd_cluster_remove,
   314→        "delete": _cmd_cluster_delete,
   315→        "move": _cmd_cluster_move,
   316→        "show": _cmd_cluster_show,
   317→        "list": _cmd_cluster_list,
   318→        "update": _cmd_cluster_update,
   319→        "merge": _cmd_cluster_merge,
   320→    }
   321→    handler = dispatch.get(cluster_action)
   322→    if handler is None:
   323→        _cmd_cluster_list(args)
   324→        return
   325→    handler(args)
   326→
   327→
   328→__all__ = ["cmd_cluster_dispatch"]
   329→

1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    ExecutionLogEntry,
     8→    PlanModel,
     9→    SkipEntry,
    10→    empty_plan,
    11→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    14→
    15→
    16→_DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES = 10000
    17→
    18→
    19→def _get_log_cap() -> int:
    20→    """Read execution_log_max_entries from config. Returns 0 for unlimited."""
    21→    try:
    22→        from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    23→
    24→        config = load_config()
    25→        value = config.get("execution_log_max_entries", _DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES)
    26→        return max(0, int(value))
    27→    except (ImportError, OSError, ValueError, TypeError):
    28→        return _DEFAULT_MAX_LOG_ENTRIES
    29→
    30→
    31→def append_log_entry(
    32→    plan: PlanModel,
    33→    action: str,
    34→    *,
    35→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    36→    cluster_name: str | None = None,
    37→    actor: str = "user",
    38→    note: str | None = None,
    39→    detail: dict | None = None,
    40→) -> None:
    41→    """Append a structured entry to the plan's execution log."""
    42→    log = plan.get("execution_log", [])
    43→    entry: ExecutionLogEntry = {
    44→        "timestamp": utc_now(),
    45→        "action": action,
    46→        "finding_ids": finding_ids or [],
    47→        "cluster_name": cluster_name,
    48→        "actor": actor,
    49→        "note": note,
    50→        "detail": detail or {},
    51→    }
    52→    log.append(entry)
    53→    cap = _get_log_cap()
    54→    if cap > 0 and len(log) > cap:
    55→        plan["execution_log"] = log[-cap:]
    56→
    57→
    58→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    59→# Helpers
    60→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    61→
    62→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    63→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    64→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    65→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    66→    if finding_id in order:
    67→        order.remove(finding_id)
    68→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    69→
    70→
    71→def _resolve_position(
    72→    order: list[str],
    73→    position: str,
    74→    target: str | None = None,
    75→    offset: int | None = None,
    76→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    77→) -> int:
    78→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    79→
    80→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    81→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    82→    """
    83→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    84→
    85→    if position == "top":
    86→        return 0
    87→    if position == "bottom":
    88→        return len(order)
    89→
    90→    if position in {"before", "after"} and target:
    91→        return _resolve_relative_position(order, moving, position=position, target=target)
    92→
    93→    if position in {"up", "down"} and offset is not None:
    94→        return _resolve_offset_position(
    95→            order,
    96→            moving,
    97→            position=position,
    98→            offset=offset,
    99→            finding_ids=finding_ids,
   100→        )
   101→
   102→    return len(order)
   103→
   104→
   105→def _resolve_relative_position(
   106→    order: list[str],
   107→    moving: set[str],
   108→    *,
   109→    position: str,
   110→    target: str,
   111→) -> int:
   112→    for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
   113→        if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
   114→            return i if position == "before" else i + 1
   115→    return 0 if position == "before" else len(order)
   116→
   117→
   118→def _find_index(items: list[str], target: str) -> int | None:
   119→    for i, item_id in enumerate(items):
   120→        if item_id == target:
   121→            return i
   122→    return None
   123→
   124→
   125→def _resolve_offset_position(
   126→    order: list[str],
   127→    moving: set[str],
   128→    *,
   129→    position: str,
   130→    offset: int,
   131→    finding_ids: list[str] | None,
   132→) -> int:
   133→    if not finding_ids:
   134→        return 0 if position == "up" else len(order)
   135→    first_id = finding_ids[0]
   136→    clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
   137→    current_idx = _find_index(clean_order, first_id)
   138→    if current_idx is None:
   139→        if position == "up":
   140→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
   141→        return len(clean_order)
   142→    if position == "up":
   143→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
   144→    return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
   145→
   146→
   147→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   148→# Move items
   149→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   150→
   151→def move_items(
   152→    plan: PlanModel,
   153→    finding_ids: list[str],
   154→    position: str,
   155→    target: str | None = None,
   156→    offset: int | None = None,
   157→) -> int:
   158→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   159→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   160→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   161→
   162→    # Remove from skipped if present
   163→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   164→    for fid in finding_ids:
   165→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   166→
   167→    # Remove from current position in order
   168→    for fid in finding_ids:
   169→        if fid in order:
   170→            order.remove(fid)
   171→
   172→    # Resolve insertion point
   173→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   174→
   175→    # Insert in original order
   176→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   177→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   178→
   179→    # Track user-promoted IDs only when explicitly moved to the front.
   180→    # Moving to bottom/down/after should not create a promotion barrier.
   181→    if position == "top":
   182→        promoted: list[str] = plan.get("promoted_ids", [])
   183→        existing_promoted = set(promoted)
   184→        for fid in finding_ids:
   185→            if fid not in existing_promoted:
   186→                promoted.append(fid)
   187→                existing_promoted.add(fid)
   188→        plan["promoted_ids"] = promoted
   189→
   190→    return len(finding_ids)
   191→
   192→
   193→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   194→# Skip / unskip
   195→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   196→
   197→def skip_items(
   198→    plan: PlanModel,
   199→    finding_ids: list[str],
   200→    *,
   201→    kind: str = "temporary",
   202→    reason: str | None = None,
   203→    note: str | None = None,
   204→    attestation: str | None = None,
   205→    review_after: int | None = None,
   206→    scan_count: int = 0,
   207→) -> int:
   208→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   209→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   210→    now = utc_now()
   211→    count = 0
   212→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   213→    skip_set = set(finding_ids)
   214→    promoted: list[str] = plan.get("promoted_ids", [])
   215→    plan["promoted_ids"] = [p for p in promoted if p not in skip_set]
   216→    for fid in finding_ids:
   217→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   218→        skipped[fid] = {
   219→            "finding_id": fid,
   220→            "kind": kind,
   221→            "reason": reason,
   222→            "note": note,
   223→            "attestation": attestation,
   224→            "created_at": now,
   225→            "review_after": review_after,
   226→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   227→        }
   228→        count += 1
   229→    return count
   230→
   231→
   232→def unskip_items(
   233→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   234→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   235→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   236→
   237→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   238→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   239→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   240→    """
   241→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   242→    count = 0
   243→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   244→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   245→    for fid in finding_ids:
   246→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   247→        if entry is not None:
   248→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   249→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   250→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   251→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   252→            count += 1
   253→    return count, need_reopen
   254→
   255→
   256→def resurface_stale_skips(
   257→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   258→) -> list[str]:
   259→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   260→
   261→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   262→    """
   263→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   264→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   265→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   266→    for fid in list(skipped):
   267→        entry = skipped[fid]
   268→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
   269→            continue
   270→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
   271→        if review_after is None:
   272→            continue
   273→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
   274→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
   275→            skipped.pop(fid)
   276→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   277→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   278→            resurfaced.append(fid)
   279→    return resurfaced
   280→
   281→
   282→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   283→# Describe / annotate
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→
   286→def describe_finding(
   287→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   288→) -> None:
   289→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""
   290→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   291→    now = utc_now()
   292→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   293→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   294→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   295→    overrides[finding_id]["description"] = description
   296→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   297→
   298→
   299→def annotate_finding(
   300→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, note: str | None
   301→) -> None:
   302→    """Set or clear a note on a finding."""
   303→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   304→    now = utc_now()
   305→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   306→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   307→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   308→    overrides[finding_id]["note"] = note
   309→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   310→
   311→
   312→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   313→# Clusters
   314→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   315→
   316→def create_cluster(
   317→    plan: PlanModel,
   318→    name: str,
   319→    description: str | None = None,
   320→    action: str | None = None,
   321→) -> Cluster:
   322→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
   323→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   324→    if name.startswith("auto/"):
   325→        raise ValueError(
   326→            f"Cluster names starting with 'auto/' are reserved for auto-clusters: {name!r}"
   327→        )
   328→    if name.startswith("epic/"):
   329→        raise ValueError(
   330→            f"Cluster names starting with 'epic/' are reserved for synthesis epics: {name!r}"
   331→        )
   332→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
   333→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
   334→    now = utc_now()
   335→    cluster: Cluster = {
   336→        "name": name,
   337→        "description": description,
   338→        "finding_ids": [],
   339→        "created_at": now,
   340→        "updated_at": now,
   341→        "auto": False,
   342→        "cluster_key": "",
   343→        "action": action,
   344→        "user_modified": False,
   345→    }
   346→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
   347→    return cluster
   348→
   349→
   350→def add_to_cluster(
   351→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   352→) -> int:
   353→    """Add finding IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
   354→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   355→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   356→    if cluster is None:
   357→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   358→
   359→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   360→    count = 0
   361→    now = utc_now()
   362→    for fid in finding_ids:
   363→        if fid not in member_ids:
   364→            member_ids.append(fid)
   365→            count += 1
   366→        # Update override to track cluster membership
   367→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   368→        if fid not in overrides:
   369→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   370→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
   371→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   372→
   373→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   374→    return count
   375→
   376→
   377→def remove_from_cluster(
   378→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   379→) -> int:
   380→    """Remove finding IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
   381→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   382→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   383→    if cluster is None:
   384→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   385→
   386→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   387→    now = utc_now()
   388→    count = 0
   389→    for fid in finding_ids:
   390→        if fid in member_ids:
   391→            member_ids.remove(fid)
   392→            count += 1
   393→        # Clear cluster from override
   394→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   395→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
   396→            override["cluster"] = None
   397→            override["updated_at"] = now
   398→
   399→    # Mark auto-clusters as user_modified when items are manually removed
   400→    if count > 0 and cluster.get("auto"):
   401→        cluster["user_modified"] = True
   402→
   403→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   404→    return count
   405→
   406→
   407→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
   408→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   409→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   410→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   411→    if cluster is None:
   412→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   413→
   414→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   415→    now = utc_now()
   416→    for fid in orphaned:
   417→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   418→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   419→            override["cluster"] = None
   420→            override["updated_at"] = now
   421→
   422→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   423→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   424→
   425→    return orphaned
   426→
   427→
   428→def merge_clusters(
   429→    plan: PlanModel, source_name: str, target_name: str
   430→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   431→    """Move all source findings to target, copy missing metadata, delete source.
   432→
   433→    Returns ``(added_count, source_finding_ids)``.
   434→    """
   435→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   436→    if source_name == target_name:
   437→        raise ValueError("Cannot merge a cluster into itself")
   438→    source = plan["clusters"].get(source_name)
   439→    if source is None:
   440→        raise ValueError(f"Source cluster {source_name!r} does not exist")
   441→    target = plan["clusters"].get(target_name)
   442→    if target is None:
   443→        raise ValueError(f"Target cluster {target_name!r} does not exist")
   444→
   445→    source_ids = list(source.get("finding_ids", []))
   446→    target_ids: list[str] = target["finding_ids"]
   447→    now = utc_now()
   448→
   449→    # Add source findings to target (deduplicate)
   450→    existing = set(target_ids)
   451→    added = 0
   452→    for fid in source_ids:
   453→        if fid not in existing:
   454→            target_ids.append(fid)
   455→            existing.add(fid)
   456→            added += 1
   457→        # Update override to point to target cluster
   458→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   459→        if fid not in overrides:
   460→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   461→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = target_name
   462→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   463→
   464→    # Copy metadata from source if target is missing them
   465→    if not target.get("description") and source.get("description"):
   466→        target["description"] = source["description"]
   467→    if not target.get("action_steps") and source.get("action_steps"):
   468→        target["action_steps"] = list(source["action_steps"])
   469→    if not target.get("action") and source.get("action"):
   470→        target["action"] = source["action"]
   471→
   472→    target["updated_at"] = now
   473→
   474→    # Delete source cluster
   475→    plan["clusters"].pop(source_name, None)
   476→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == source_name:
   477→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   478→
   479→    return added, source_ids
   480→
   481→
   482→def move_cluster(
   483→    plan: PlanModel,
   484→    cluster_name: str,
   485→    position: str,
   486→    target: str | None = None,
   487→    offset: int | None = None,
   488→) -> int:
   489→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   490→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   491→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   492→    if cluster is None:
   493→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   494→
   495→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   496→    if not member_ids:
   497→        return 0
   498→
   499→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   500→
   501→
   502→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   503→# Focus
   504→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   505→
   506→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
   507→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
   508→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   509→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
   510→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   511→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
   512→
   513→
   514→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   515→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
   516→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   517→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
   518→
   519→
   520→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   521→# Reset
   522→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   523→
   524→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   525→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp."""
   526→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
   527→    plan.clear()
   528→    for k, v in empty_plan().items():
   529→        plan[k] = v
   530→    plan["created"] = created
   531→
   532→
   533→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
   534→    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
   535→
   536→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
   537→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
   538→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
   539→    """
   540→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   541→    found = 0
   542→
   543→    purge_set = set(finding_ids)
   544→    promoted: list[str] = plan.get("promoted_ids", [])
   545→    plan["promoted_ids"] = [p for p in promoted if p not in purge_set]
   546→
   547→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   548→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   549→    for fid in finding_ids:
   550→        was_present = False
   551→        if fid in order:
   552→            order.remove(fid)
   553→            was_present = True
   554→        if fid in skipped:
   555→            skipped.pop(fid)
   556→            was_present = True
   557→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   558→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   559→            if fid in ids:
   560→                ids.remove(fid)
   561→                was_present = True
   562→        # Clear stale cluster reference from override (preserve notes/descriptions)
   563→        override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(fid)
   564→        if override and override.get("cluster"):
   565→            override["cluster"] = None
   566→            override["updated_at"] = utc_now()
   567→        if was_present:
   568→            found += 1
   569→
   570→    return found
   571→
   572→
   573→__all__ = [
   574→    "add_to_cluster",
   575→    "annotate_finding",
   576→    "append_log_entry",
   577→    "clear_focus",
   578→    "create_cluster",
   579→    "delete_cluster",
   580→    "describe_finding",
   581→    "merge_clusters",
   582→    "move_cluster",

   591→]

    38→def _show_concerns(state: dict, lang_name: str | None) -> None:
    39→    """Render current design concerns from mechanical signals."""
    40→    concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang_name)
    41→    if not concerns:
    42→        print(colorize("  No design concerns detected.", "green"))
    43→        return
    44→

    46→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})

    48→    for i, c in enumerate(concerns, 1):
    49→        print(colorize(f"  {i}. [{c.type}] {c.file}", "cyan"))
    50→        print(f"     {c.summary}")
    51→        for ev in c.evidence:
    52→            print(colorize(f"       - {ev}", "dim"))
    53→        print(colorize(f"     ? {c.question}", "yellow"))
    54→
    55→        # Check if previously dismissed (but resurface due to changed findings).
    56→        prev = dismissals.get(c.fingerprint)
    57→        if isinstance(prev, dict):
    58→            reasoning = prev.get("reasoning", "")
    59→            if reasoning:
    60→                print(colorize(f"     (previously dismissed: {reasoning})", "dim"))

    67→    strict_val = (

   119→def _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state: dict, entity) -> None:
   120→    """Show score + 'no open findings' for a mechanical dimension with zero findings."""
   121→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, entity.display_name)
   122→    _print_dimension_score(dim_data, display_name)

   132→def _load_dimension_findings(

   136→) -> list[dict]:
   137→    """Load findings for all detectors in a mechanical dimension."""
   138→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
   139→    for detector in entity.detectors:
   140→        matches = load_matches(

   142→        )
   143→        all_matches.extend(matches)

   145→    seen: set[str] = set()

   149→        if fid not in seen:
   150→            seen.add(fid)
   151→            unique.append(item)
   152→    return unique

   194→        print(colorize("    `show subjective_review --status open`  Files needing re-review", "dim"))
   195→
   196→
   197→def cmd_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   198→    """Show all findings for a file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
   199→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   200→    state = runtime.state
   201→    config = runtime.config
   202→
   203→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   204→        return
   205→
   206→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
   207→    if stale_warning:
   208→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
   209→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
   210→    if skill_warning:
   211→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
   212→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
   213→    if config_warning:
   214→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   215→
   216→    print_synthesis_guardrail_info(state=state)
   217→
   218→    pattern_raw = getattr(args, "pattern", "")
   219→    show_code = getattr(args, "code", False)
   220→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
   221→    ok, pattern, status_filter, scope = resolve_show_scope(args)
   222→    if not ok or pattern is None:
   223→        return
   224→
   225→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   226→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   227→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   228→        state,
   229→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="show"),
   230→    )
   231→
   232→    # --- Entity resolution: classify what the user asked for ---
   233→    entity = resolve_entity(pattern, state)
   234→
   235→    # Dispatch: concerns special view
   236→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() == "concerns":
   237→        _show_concerns(state, lang_name)
   238→        return
   239→
   240→    # Dispatch: subjective dimension
   241→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and entity.is_subjective:
   242→        _render_subjective_dimension(state, config, entity, pattern_raw)
   243→        return
   244→
   245→    # Dispatch: mechanical dimension
   246→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and not entity.is_subjective:
   247→        matches = _load_dimension_findings(state, entity, status_filter)
   248→        if not matches:
   249→            _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state, entity)
   250→            return
   251→        pattern = entity.display_name
   252→    else:
   253→        # All other kinds: existing load_matches path
   254→        matches = load_matches(
   255→            state, scope=scope, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=chronic
   256→        )
   257→
   258→    if not matches:
   259→        _render_no_matches(entity, pattern, status_filter, narrative, state, config)
   260→        return
   261→
   262→    no_budget = getattr(args, "no_budget", False)
   263→    (
   264→        surfaced_matches,
   265→        hidden_by_detector,
   266→        noise_budget,
   267→        global_noise_budget,
   268→        budget_warning,
   269→    ) = resolve_noise(
   270→        config,
   271→        matches,
   272→        no_budget=no_budget,
   273→    )
   274→    hidden_total = sum(hidden_by_detector.values())
   275→
   276→    payload = build_show_payload(
   277→        surfaced_matches,
   278→        pattern,
   279→        status_filter,
   280→        ShowPayloadMeta(
   281→            total_matches=len(matches),
   282→            hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   283→            noise_budget=noise_budget,
   284→            global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   285→        ),
   286→    )
   287→    write_query({"command": "show", **payload, "narrative": narrative})
   288→
   289→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   290→    if output_file:
   291→        if write_show_output_file(output_file, payload, len(surfaced_matches)):
   292→            return
   293→        raise SystemExit(1)
   294→
   295→    top = getattr(args, "top", 20) or 20
   296→    render_findings(
   297→        surfaced_matches,
   298→        pattern=pattern,
   299→        status_filter=status_filter,
   300→        show_code=show_code,
   301→        top=top,
   302→        hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   303→        hidden_total=hidden_total,
   304→        noise_budget=noise_budget,
   305→        global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   306→        budget_warning=budget_warning,
   307→    )
   308→    try:
   309→        _plan = load_plan()
   310→        _plan_active = _plan if (_plan.get("queue_order") or _plan.get("clusters")) else None
   311→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
   312→        _plan_active = None
   313→    show_agent_plan(narrative, surfaced_matches, plan=_plan_active)
   314→    show_subjective_followup(
   315→        state,
   316→        target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   317→    )
   318→
   319→    _render_subjective_views_guide(entity)
   320→
   321→
   322→__all__ = ["cmd_show"]
   323→
