```
1→# Desloppify - an agent harness to make your codebase 🤌
     2→
     3→[![PyPI version](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/desloppify)](https://pypi.org/project/desloppify/) ![Python 3.11+](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11%2B-blue)
     4→
     5→Desloppify gives your AI coding agent the tools to identify, understand, and systematically improve codebase quality. It combines mechanical detection (dead code, duplication, complexity) with subjective LLM review (naming, abstractions, module boundaries), then works through a prioritized fix loop. State persists across scans so it chips away over multiple sessions, and the scoring is designed to resist gaming.
     6→
     7→<img src="assets/explained.png" width="100%">
     8→
     9→The score gives your agent a north-star, and the tooling helps it plan, execute, and resolve issues until it hits your target — with a lot of tricks to keep it on track. A score above 98 should correlate with a codebase a seasoned engineer would call beautiful.
    10→
    11→That score generates a scorecard badge for your GitHub profile or README:
    12→
    13→<img src="assets/scorecard.png" width="100%">
    14→
    15→Currently supports 28 languages — full plugin depth for TypeScript, Python, C#, Dart, GDScript, and Go; generic linter + tree-sitter support for Rust, Ruby, Java, Kotlin, and 17 more.
    16→
    17→## For your agent's consideration...
    18→
    19→Paste this prompt into your agent:
    20→
    21→```
    22→I want you to improve the quality of this codebase. To do this, install and run desloppify.
    23→Run ALL of the following (requires Python 3.11+):
    24→
    25→pip install --upgrade "desloppify[full]"
    26→desloppify update-skill claude    # installs the full workflow guide — pick yours: claude, cursor, codex, copilot, windsurf, gemini
    27→desloppify scan --path .
    28→desloppify next
    29→
    30→--path is the directory to scan (use "." for the whole project, or "src/" etc).
    31→
    32→Your goal is to get the strict score as high as possible. The scoring resists gaming — the
    33→only way to improve it is to actually make the code better.
    34→
    35→THE LOOP: run `next`. It tells you what to fix, which file, and the resolve command to run
    36→when done. Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Over and over. This is your main job.
    37→
    38→Don't be lazy. Large refactors and small detailed fixes — do both with equal energy. No task
    39→is too big or too small. Fix things properly, not minimally.
    40→
    41→Use `plan` to reorder priorities or cluster related issues. Rescan periodically. The scan
    42→output includes agent instructions — follow them, don't substitute your own analysis.
    43→```
    44→
    45→## From Vibe Coding to Vibe Engineering
    46→
    47→Vibe coding gets things built fast. But the codebases it produces tend to rot in ways that are hard to see and harder to fix — not just the mechanical stuff like dead imports, but the structural kind. Abstractions that made sense at first stop making sense. Naming drifts. Error handling is done three different ways. The codebase works, but working in it gets worse over time.
    48→
    49→LLMs are actually good at spotting this now, if you ask them the right questions. That's the core bet here — that an agent with the right framework can hold a codebase to a real standard, the kind that used to require a senior engineer paying close attention over months.
    50→
    51→So we're trying to define what "good" looks like as a score that's actually worth optimizing. Not a lint score you game to 100 by suppressing warnings. Something where improving the number means the codebase genuinely got better. That's hard, and we're not done, but the anti-gaming stuff matters to us a lot — it's the difference between a useful signal and a vanity metric.
    52→
    53→The hope is that anyone can use this to build something a seasoned engineer would look at and respect. That's the bar we're aiming for.
    54→
    55→If you'd like to join a community of vibe engineers who want to build beautiful things, [come hang out](https://discord.gg/aZdzbZrHaY).
    56→
    57→<img src="assets/engineering.png" width="100%">
    58→
    59→---
    60→
    61→<details>
    62→<summary><strong>Stuff you probably won't need to know</strong></summary>
    63→
    64→#### Commands
    65→
    66→| Command | Description |
    67→|---------|-------------|
    68→| `scan [--reset-subjective]` | Run all detectors, update state (optional: reset subjective baseline to 0 first) |
    69→| `status` | Score + per-tier progress |
    70→| `show <pattern>` | Findings by file, directory, detector, or ID |
    71→| `next [--tier N] [--explain]` | Highest-priority open finding (--explain: with score context) |
    72→| `resolve <status> <patterns>` | Mark open / fixed / wontfix / false_positive |
    73→| `fix <fixer> [--dry-run]` | Auto-fix mechanical issues |
    74→| `review --prepare` | Generate subjective review packet (`query.json`) |
    75→| `review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel` | Run blind subjective batch assessments, merge/import, optionally `--scan-after-import` |
    76→| `review --import <file> [--allow-partial]` | Import subjective review findings (fails closed on invalid findings by default) |
    77→| `review --external-start --external-runner claude` | Start Claude cloud blind-review session (creates session/token/template) |
    78→| `review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>` | Submit Claude session output (from generated template); CLI injects canonical provenance |
    79→| `next [--tier N] [--cluster <name>]` | Next priority item (respects plan, shows clusters) |
    80→| `zone` | Show/set/clear zone classifications |
    81→| `config` | Show/set/unset project configuration |
    82→| `move <src> <dst>` | Move file/directory, update all imports |
    83→| `detect <name>` | Run a single detector raw |
    84→| `plan` | Prioritized markdown plan |
    85→| `tree` | Annotated codebase tree |
    86→| `viz` | Interactive HTML treemap |
    87→| `dev scaffold-lang` | Generate a standardized language plugin scaffold |
    88→
    89→#### Subjective Import Guardrails
    90→
    91→- Findings must match `query.json` / packet `system_prompt` schema exactly:
    92→  `dimension`, `identifier`, `summary`, `related_files`, `evidence`, `suggestion`, `confidence`.
    93→- Score/feedback consistency is enforced:
    94→  - scores below `100.0` require explicit same-dimension feedback (finding suggestion or `dimension_notes` evidence)
    95→  - scores below `85.0` require at least one same-dimension finding
    96→- `desloppify review --import` is fail-closed: if any finding is invalid/skipped, the import aborts and state is not saved.
    97→- Use `--allow-partial` only when you explicitly want to accept skipped findings.
    98→
    99→#### Review Batch Runtime Logs
   100→
```

  1515→        _confirm_reflect(args, plan, stages, attestation)
  1516→    elif confirm_stage == "organize":

  1524→def _cmd_synthesize_start(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
  1525→    """Manually inject ``synthesis::pending`` into the queue and clear prior stages."""
  1526→    plan = load_plan()
  1527→    order: list[str] = plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
  1528→
  1529→    if SYNTHESIS_ID in order:
  1530→        print(colorize("  synthesis::pending is already in the queue.", "yellow"))
  1531→        meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
  1532→        stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
  1533→        if stages:
  1534→            print(colorize(f"  {len(stages)} stage(s) in progress — clearing to restart.", "yellow"))
  1535→            meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}
  1536→            save_plan(plan)
  1537→            append_log_entry(plan, "synthesis_start", actor="user",
  1538→                             detail={"action": "restart", "cleared_stages": list(stages.keys())})
  1539→            save_plan(plan)
  1540→            print(colorize("  Stages cleared. Begin with observe:", "green"))
  1541→        else:
  1542→            print(colorize("  Begin with observe:", "green"))
  1543→        print(colorize(f"    {SYNTH_CMD_OBSERVE}", "dim"))
  1544→        return
  1545→
  1546→    # Inject at front
  1547→    order.insert(0, SYNTHESIS_ID)
  1548→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
  1549→    meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}
  1550→    save_plan(plan)
  1551→
  1552→    append_log_entry(plan, "synthesis_start", actor="user",
  1553→                     detail={"action": "start"})
  1554→    save_plan(plan)
  1555→
  1556→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
  1557→    si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, runtime.state)
  1558→    print(colorize("  Synthesis started.", "green"))
  1559→    print(f"  Open review findings: {len(si.open_findings)}")
  1560→    print(colorize("  Begin with observe:", "dim"))
  1561→    print(colorize(f"    {SYNTH_CMD_OBSERVE}", "dim"))

1→"""Shared display utilities for command output."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
     7→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
     8→
     9→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    10→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    11→    """
    12→    if "::" in fid:
    13→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    14→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    15→            return suffix[:8]
    16→    return fid
    17→
    18→
    19→__all__ = ["short_finding_id"]
    20→
```

1→"""Epic synthesis engine — LLM-driven meta-plan for review findings.
     2→
     3→Clusters review findings by root cause, dismisses false positives,
     4→resolves contradictions, and orders by dependency. The synthesis is incremental:
     5→it updates existing synthesis-clusters rather than recreating from scratch.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→

    33→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→# Finding-ID citation extraction
    35→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→
    37→FINDING_ID_RE = re.compile(r"[a-z_]+::[a-f0-9]{8,}")
    38→
    39→
    40→def extract_finding_citations(text: str, valid_ids: set[str]) -> set[str]:
    41→    """Extract finding IDs cited in free text.
    42→
    43→    Matches full finding IDs (e.g. ``review::abcdef12``) or bare 8+ char
    44→    hex suffixes that correspond to a known finding.
    45→    """
    46→    cited: set[str] = set()
    47→    # Match full finding IDs
    48→    for match in FINDING_ID_RE.finditer(text):
    49→        candidate = match.group()
    50→        if candidate in valid_ids:
    51→            cited.add(candidate)
    52→    # Match 8+ char hex suffixes
    53→    for token in re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", text):
    54→        for valid_id in valid_ids:
    55→            if valid_id.endswith("::" + token):
    56→                cited.add(valid_id)
    57→                break
    58→    return cited
    59→
    60→
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→# Completed-work helpers
    63→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    64→
    65→def last_real_review_timestamp(state: dict) -> str | None:
    66→    """ISO timestamp of most recent genuine review import (not manual override/scan reset)."""
    67→    REAL_MODES = {"holistic", "per_file", "trusted_internal", "attested_external"}
    68→    audit = state.get("assessment_import_audit", [])
    69→    if isinstance(audit, list):
    70→        for entry in reversed(audit):
    71→            if isinstance(entry, dict) and entry.get("mode") in REAL_MODES:
    72→                ts = entry.get("timestamp")
    73→                if ts:
    74→                    return str(ts)
    75→    holistic = (state.get("review_cache") or {}).get("holistic")
    76→    if isinstance(holistic, dict):
    77→        return holistic.get("reviewed_at")
    78→    return None
    79→
    80→

    82→    open_findings: dict[str, dict],
    83→    resolved_findings: dict[str, dict],
    84→) -> dict[str, dict]:
    85→    """Detect dimensions with both resolved AND current open findings.
    86→
    87→    Returns ``{dimension: {"open": [ids], "resolved": [ids]}}``.
    88→    A dimension with both resolved and open findings signals a potential
    89→    loop — similar issues recur after previous fixes.
    90→    """
    91→    def _dimension(f: dict) -> str:
    92→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
    93→        if isinstance(detail, dict):
    94→            return detail.get("dimension", "")
    95→        return ""
    96→

    37→def _scorecard_subjective(
    38→    state: dict,
    39→    dim_scores: dict,
    40→) -> list[dict]:
    41→    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective entries for current dimension scores."""
    42→    return next_render_mod.scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _low_subjective_dimensions(
    46→    state: dict,
    47→    dim_scores: dict,
    48→    *,
    49→    threshold: float = 95.0,
    50→) -> list[tuple[str, float, int]]:
    51→    """Return assessed scorecard-subjective entries below the threshold."""
    52→    low: list[tuple[str, float, int]] = []
    53→    for entry in _scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores):
    54→        if entry.get("placeholder"):
    55→            continue
    56→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
    57→        if strict_val < threshold:
    58→            low.append(
    59→                (
    60→                    str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
    61→                    strict_val,
    62→                    int(entry.get("issues", 0)),
    63→                )
    64→            )
    65→    low.sort(key=lambda item: item[1])
    66→    return low
    67→
    68→
    69→def cmd_next(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    70→    """Show next highest-priority queue items."""
    71→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    72→    state = runtime.state
    73→    config = runtime.config
    74→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    75→        return
    76→
    77→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    78→    if stale_warning:
    79→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    80→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    81→    if skill_warning:
    82→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    83→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
    84→    if config_warning:
    85→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
    86→
    87→    print_synthesis_guardrail_info(state=state)
    88→    _get_items(args, state, config)

81→def detect_recurring_patterns(
    82→    open_findings: dict[str, dict],
    83→    resolved_findings: dict[str, dict],
    84→) -> dict[str, dict]:
    85→    """Detect dimensions with both resolved AND current open findings.
    86→
    87→    Returns ``{dimension: {"open": [ids], "resolved": [ids]}}``.
    88→    A dimension with both resolved and open findings signals a potential
    89→    loop — similar issues recur after previous fixes.
    90→    """
    91→    def _dimension(f: dict) -> str:
    92→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
    93→        if isinstance(detail, dict):
    94→            return detail.get("dimension", "")
    95→        return ""
    96→
    97→    open_by_dim: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    98→    for fid, f in open_findings.items():
    99→        dim = _dimension(f)
   100→        if dim:
   101→            open_by_dim.setdefault(dim, []).append(fid)
   102→
   103→    resolved_by_dim: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   104→    for fid, f in resolved_findings.items():
   105→        dim = _dimension(f)
   106→        if dim:
   107→            resolved_by_dim.setdefault(dim, []).append(fid)
   108→
   109→    recurring: dict[str, dict] = {}
   110→    for dim in set(open_by_dim) & set(resolved_by_dim):
   111→        recurring[dim] = {
   112→            "open": open_by_dim[dim],
   113→            "resolved": resolved_by_dim[dim],
   114→        }
   115→    return recurring
   116→
   117→
   118→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   119→# Data types
   120→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   121→
   122→@dataclass
   123→class SynthesisInput:
   124→    """All data the LLM needs to produce/update epics."""
   125→
   126→    open_findings: dict[str, dict]       # id -> finding (review + concerns)
   127→    mechanical_findings: dict[str, dict]  # id -> finding (non-review, for context)
   128→    existing_epics: dict[str, Cluster]    # current synthesis-clusters to update
   129→    dimension_scores: dict[str, Any]      # for context
   130→    new_since_last: set[str]             # finding IDs new since last synthesis
   131→    resolved_since_last: set[str]        # finding IDs resolved since last
   132→    previously_dismissed: list[str]      # IDs dismissed in prior synthesis
   133→    synthesis_version: int               # next version number
   134→    resolved_findings: dict[str, dict]   # full finding objects for resolved IDs
   135→    completed_clusters: list[dict]       # clusters completed since last synthesis
   136→
   137→
   138→@dataclass
   139→class DismissedFinding:
   140→    """A finding the LLM says doesn't make sense."""
   141→
   142→    finding_id: str
   143→    reason: str
   144→
   145→
   146→@dataclass
   147→class ContradictionNote:
   148→    """Record of a resolved contradiction."""
   149→
   150→    kept: str
   151→    dismissed: str
   152→    reason: str
   153→
   154→
   155→@dataclass
   156→class SynthesisResult:
   157→    """Parsed and validated LLM synthesis output."""
   158→
   159→    strategy_summary: str
   160→    epics: list[dict]

    26→def detail_dict(item: dict) -> dict:
    27→    """Return finding detail as a dict; tolerate legacy/non-dict payloads."""
    28→    detail = item.get("detail")
    29→    return detail if isinstance(detail, dict) else {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def status_matches(item_status: str, status_filter: str) -> bool:
    33→    return status_filter == "all" or item_status == status_filter
    34→
    35→
    36→def is_subjective_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    37→    detector = item.get("detector")
    38→    if detector in {"subjective_assessment"}:
    39→        return True
    40→    if detector == "holistic_review":
    41→        return True
    42→    return False
    43→
    44→
    45→def is_review_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    46→    return item.get("detector") == "review"
    47→
    48→
    49→def review_finding_weight(item: dict) -> float:
    50→    """Return review issue weight aligned with issues list ordering."""
    51→    confidence = str(item.get("confidence", "low")).lower()
    52→    weight_by_confidence = {
    53→        "high": 1.0,
    54→        "medium": 0.7,
    55→        "low": 0.3,
    56→    }
    57→    weight = weight_by_confidence.get(confidence, 0.3)
    58→    if detail_dict(item).get("holistic"):
    59→        weight *= 10.0
    60→    return float(weight)
    61→
    62→
    63→def scope_matches(item: dict, scope: str | None) -> bool:
    64→    """Apply show-style pattern matching against a queue item."""
    65→    if not scope:
    66→        return True
    67→
    68→    item_id = item.get("id", "")
    69→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    70→    filepath = item.get("file", "")
    71→    summary = item.get("summary", "")
    72→    dimension = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    73→    kind = item.get("kind", "")
    74→
    75→    if "*" in scope:
    76→        return any(
    77→            fnmatch(candidate, scope)
    78→            for candidate in (item_id, filepath, detector, dimension, summary)
    79→        )
    80→
    81→    if "::" in scope:
    82→        return item_id.startswith(scope)
    83→
    84→    lowered = scope.lower()
    85→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
    86→        return (
    87→            lowered in item_id.lower()
    88→            or lowered in dimension.lower()
    89→            or lowered in summary.lower()
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    # Hash suffix: 8+ hex chars matches the tail segment of a finding ID.
    93→    if len(lowered) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]+", lowered):
    94→        return item_id.lower().endswith("::" + lowered)
    95→
    96→    return (
    97→        detector == scope
    98→        or filepath == scope
    99→        or filepath.startswith(scope.rstrip("/") + "/")
   100→    )

   102→
   103→def slugify(text: str) -> str:
   104→    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9_]+", "_", text.lower()).strip("_")
   105→
   106→
   107→def _canonical_subjective_dimension_key(display_name: str) -> str:
   108→    """Map a display label (e.g. 'Mid elegance') to its canonical dimension key."""
   109→    cleaned = display_name.replace(" (subjective)", "").strip()
   110→    target = cleaned.lower()
   111→
   112→    for dim_key, label in DISPLAY_NAMES.items():
   113→        if str(label).lower() == target:
   114→            return str(dim_key)
   115→    return slugify(cleaned)
   116→
   117→
   118→def _subjective_dimension_aliases(display_name: str) -> set[str]:
   119→    """Return normalized aliases used to match display labels with finding dimension keys."""
   120→    cleaned = display_name.replace(" (subjective)", "").strip()
   121→    canonical = _canonical_subjective_dimension_key(cleaned)
   122→    return {
   123→        cleaned.lower(),
   124→        cleaned.replace(" ", "_").lower(),
   125→        slugify(cleaned),
   126→        canonical.lower(),
   127→        slugify(canonical),
   128→    }

   131→def supported_fixers_for_item(state: dict, item: dict) -> set[str] | None:
   132→    """Return supported fixers for an item's language when known."""
   133→    lang = str(item.get("lang", "") or "").strip()
   134→    if not lang:
   135→        return None
   136→
   137→    caps = state.get("lang_capabilities", {})
   138→    if not isinstance(caps, dict):
   139→        return None
   140→
   141→    lang_caps = caps.get(lang, {})
   142→    if not isinstance(lang_caps, dict):
   143→        return None
   144→
   145→    fixers = lang_caps.get("fixers")
   146→    if not isinstance(fixers, list):
   147→        return None
   148→    return {fixer for fixer in fixers if isinstance(fixer, str)}
   149→

   151→def primary_command_for_finding(
   152→    item: dict, *, supported_fixers: set[str] | None = None
   153→) -> str:
   154→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   155→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   156→    if meta and meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and meta.fixers:
   157→        available_fixers = [
   158→            fixer
   159→            for fixer in meta.fixers
   160→            if supported_fixers is not None and fixer in supported_fixers
   161→        ]
   162→        if available_fixers:
   163→            return f"desloppify fix {available_fixers[0]} --dry-run"
   164→    if detector == "subjective_review":
   165→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(item):
   166→            return "desloppify review --prepare"
   167→        return "desloppify show subjective"
   168→    return f'desloppify plan done "{item.get("id", "")}" --note "<what you did>" --confirm'

   171→def build_synthesis_item(plan: dict, state: dict) -> dict | None:
   172→    """Build a synthetic T1 work item for ``synthesis::pending`` if it's in the queue.
   173→
   174→    Returns ``None`` when synthesis is not pending.
   175→    """

   186→        if f.get("status") == "open"
   187→        and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   188→    }
   189→    synthesized_ids = set(meta.get("synthesized_ids", []))
   190→    new_since = len(open_review_ids - synthesized_ids)
   191→    resolved_since = len(synthesized_ids - open_review_ids)
   192→
   193→    return {
   194→        "id": SYNTHESIS_ID,
   195→        "tier": 1,
   196→        "confidence": "high",
   197→        "detector": "synthesis",

   204→        "detail": {
   205→            "total_review_findings": len(open_review_ids),
   206→            "new_since_last": new_since,
   207→            "resolved_since_last": resolved_since,
   208→        },
   209→    }

   222→            continue
   223→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
   224→        [REDACTED](name)
   225→        aliases = _subjective_dimension_aliases(name)
   226→        for cli_key in entry.get("cli_keys", []):
   227→            key = str(cli_key).strip().lower()
   228→            if not key:
   229→                continue
   230→            aliases.add(key)
   231→            aliases.add(slugify(key))
   232→        aliases.add(dim_key.lower())

   234→        for alias in aliases:

   236→    return scores

   238→
   239→def build_subjective_items(
   240→    state: dict, findings: dict, *, threshold: float = 100.0
   241→) -> list[dict]:
   242→    """Create synthetic subjective work items (always tier 4)."""
   243→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
   244→    if not dim_scores:
   245→        return []
   246→    threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, float(threshold)))
   247→
   248→    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective_entries(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)
   249→    if not subjective_entries:
   250→        return []
   251→    unassessed_dims = {
   252→        str(name).strip()
   253→        for name in unassessed_subjective_dimensions(
   254→            dim_scores
   255→        )
   256→    }
   257→
   258→    # Review findings are keyed by raw dimension name (snake_case).
   259→    review_open_by_dim: dict[str, int] = {}
   260→    for finding in findings.values():
   261→        if finding.get("status") != "open" or finding.get("detector") != "review":
   262→            continue
   263→        dim_key = str(detail_dict(finding).get("dimension", "")).strip().lower()
   264→        if not dim_key:
   265→            continue
   266→        review_open_by_dim[dim_key] = review_open_by_dim.get(dim_key, 0) + 1
   267→
   268→    items: list[dict] = []
   269→    def _prepare_command(
   270→        cli_keys: list[str],
   271→        *,
   272→        force_review_rerun: bool = False,
   273→    ) -> str:
   274→        command = "desloppify review --prepare"
   275→        if cli_keys:
   276→            command += " --dimensions " + ",".join(cli_keys)
   277→        if force_review_rerun:
   278→            command += " --force-review-rerun"
   279→        return command
   280→
   281→    for entry in subjective_entries:
   282→        name = str(entry.get("name", "")).strip()
   283→        if not name:
   284→            continue
   285→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
   286→        if strict_val >= threshold:
   287→            continue
   288→
   289→        [REDACTED](name)
   290→        aliases = set(_subjective_dimension_aliases(name))
   291→        cli_keys = [
   292→            str(key).strip().lower()
   293→            for key in entry.get("cli_keys", [])
   294→            if str(key).strip()
   295→        ]
   296→        aliases.update(cli_keys)
   297→        aliases.update(slugify(key) for key in cli_keys)
   298→        open_review = sum(review_open_by_dim.get(alias, 0) for alias in aliases)
   299→        is_unassessed = bool(entry.get("placeholder")) or (
   300→            name in unassessed_dims
   301→            or (strict_val <= 0.0 and int(entry.get("issues", 0)) == 0)
   302→        )
   303→        is_stale = bool(entry.get("stale"))
   304→        # Items whose only action is --force-review-rerun are gated by the
   305→        # review preflight while objective backlog exists.  Flag them so the
   306→        # queue builder can suppress them until the backlog drains.
   307→        is_backlog_gated = open_review == 0 and not is_unassessed
   308→        # If review findings already exist for this dimension, triage/fix them
   309→        # before suggesting another review refresh pass.
   310→        if open_review > 0:
   311→            primary_command = "desloppify show review --status open"
   312→        elif is_unassessed:
   313→            primary_command = _prepare_command(cli_keys)
   314→        elif is_stale:
   315→            primary_command = _prepare_command(
   316→                cli_keys,
   317→                force_review_rerun=True,
   318→            )
   319→        elif cli_keys:
   320→            primary_command = _prepare_command(
   321→                cli_keys,
   322→                force_review_rerun=True,
   323→            )
   324→        else:
   325→            primary_command = _prepare_command(
   326→                cli_keys,
   327→                force_review_rerun=True,
   328→            )
   329→        stale_tag = " [stale — re-review]" if is_stale else ""
   330→        summary = f"Subjective dimension below target: {name} ({strict_val:.1f}%){stale_tag}"
   331→        items.append(
   332→            {
   333→                "id": f"subjective::{slugify(dim_key)}",
   334→                "detector": "subjective_assessment",
   335→                "file": ".",
   336→                "tier": 4,
   337→                "effective_tier": 4,
   338→                "confidence": "medium",
   339→                "summary": summary,
   340→                "detail": {
   341→                    "dimension_name": name,
   342→                    "dimension": dim_key,
   343→                    "issues": int(entry.get("issues", 0)),
   344→                    "strict_score": strict_val,
   345→                    "open_review_findings": open_review,
   346→                    "cli_keys": cli_keys,
   347→                },
   348→                "status": "open",
   349→                "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   350→                "primary_command": primary_command,
   351→                "backlog_gated": is_backlog_gated,
   352→            }
   353→        )
   354→    return items

    20→def _state_with_findings(*ids: str, dimension: str = "naming") -> dict:
    21→    """Build minimal state with open review findings in a given dimension."""
    22→    findings = {}
    23→    for fid in ids:
    24→        findings[fid] = {
    25→            "status": "open",
    26→            "detector": "review",
    27→            "file": "test.py",
    28→            "summary": f"Review finding {fid}",
    29→            "confidence": "medium",
    30→            "tier": 2,
    31→            "detail": {"dimension": dimension},
    32→        }
    33→    return {"findings": findings, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    34→
    35→
    36→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    37→    plan = empty_plan()
    38→    plan["queue_order"] = [SYNTHESIS_ID]
    39→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    40→    stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
    41→    for name in stage_names:
    42→        stages[name] = {
    43→            "stage": name,
    44→            "report": f"A sufficiently long report for {name} stage that meets minimum length requirements and more text",
    45→            "cited_ids": [],
    46→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    47→            "finding_count": 5,
    48→        }
    49→        if confirmed:
    50→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    51→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = "I have thoroughly reviewed all the findings in this stage"
    52→    return plan

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Input collection
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def collect_synthesis_input(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> SynthesisInput:
    """Gather all data needed for the synthesis LLM prompt."""
    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    epics = synthesis_clusters(plan)

    open_review: dict[str, dict] = {}
    open_mechanical: dict[str, dict] = {}
    for fid, f in findings.items():
        if f.get("status") != "open":
            continue
        if f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns"):
            open_review[fid] = f
        else:
            open_mechanical[fid] = f

    synthesized_ids = set(meta.get("synthesized_ids", []))
    current_review_ids = set(open_review.keys())
    new_since = current_review_ids - synthesized_ids
    resolved_since = synthesized_ids - current_review_ids
--
    call the LLM or mutate the plan.

    If ``deps.llm_call`` is None, returns a dry-run result with the prompt.
    """
    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, state)

    prompt = build_synthesis_prompt(si)
    valid_ids = set(si.open_findings.keys())

    if dry_run or deps is None or deps.llm_call is None:
        result = SynthesisMutationResult(dry_run=True)
        result.strategy_summary = f"[dry-run] Prompt built with {len(si.open_findings)} findings"
        return result

    # Call LLM
    try:
        raw_response = deps.llm_call(_SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT, prompt)
        raw_json = json.loads(raw_response)
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
        logger.error("Epic synthesis LLM response parse error: %s", exc)
        result = SynthesisMutationResult()
        result.strategy_summary = f"Synthesis failed: {exc}"
        return result

    synthesis = parse_synthesis_result(raw_json, valid_ids)
--
    "SynthesisInput",
    "SynthesisMutationResult",
    "SynthesisResult",
    "apply_synthesis_to_plan",
    "build_synthesis_prompt",
    "collect_synthesis_input",
    "detect_recurring_patterns",
    "extract_finding_citations",
    "last_real_review_timestamp",
    "parse_synthesis_result",
    "synthesize_epics",
]
```

1→"""Shared display utilities for command output."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
     7→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
     8→
     9→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    10→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    11→    """
    12→    if "::" in fid:
    13→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    14→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    15→            return suffix[:8]
    16→    return fid
    17→
    18→
    19→__all__ = ["short_finding_id"]
    20→

1→"""Handler for ``plan synthesize`` subcommand.
     2→

     4→with a fast-track CONFIRM-EXISTING skip path.
     5→
     6→Stage gates validate **plan data enrichment**, not text reports:
     7→- OBSERVE: lightweight — write an analysis of themes/root causes (100 char min)
     8→- REFLECT: compare current findings against completed work (recurring patterns)

1568→def cmd_plan_synthesize(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
  1569→    """Run epic synthesis: staged workflow OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → COMMIT."""
  1570→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
  1571→    state = runtime.state
  1572→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
  1573→        return
  1574→
  1575→    # Route: --start
  1576→    if getattr(args, "start", False):
  1577→        _cmd_synthesize_start(args)
  1578→        return
  1579→
  1580→    # Route: --confirm observe/reflect/organize
  1581→    if getattr(args, "confirm", None):
  1582→        _cmd_confirm_stage(args)
  1583→        return
  1584→
  1585→    # Route: --complete
  1586→    if getattr(args, "complete", False):
  1587→        _cmd_synthesize_complete(args)
  1588→        return
  1589→
  1590→    # Route: --confirm-existing
  1591→    if getattr(args, "confirm_existing", False):
  1592→        _cmd_confirm_existing(args)
  1593→        return
  1594→
  1595→    # Route: --stage observe/reflect/organize
  1596→    stage = getattr(args, "stage", None)
  1597→    if stage == "observe":
  1598→        _cmd_stage_observe(args)
  1599→        return
  1600→    if stage == "reflect":
  1601→        _cmd_stage_reflect(args)
  1602→        return
  1603→    if stage == "organize":
  1604→        _cmd_stage_organize(args)
  1605→        return
  1606→
  1607→    # Dry-run mode
  1608→    if getattr(args, "dry_run", False):
  1609→        plan = load_plan()
  1610→        si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, state)
  1611→        prompt = build_synthesis_prompt(si)
  1612→        print(colorize("  Epic synthesis \u2014 dry run", "bold"))
  1613→        print(colorize("  " + "\u2500" * 60, "dim"))
  1614→        print(f"  Open review findings: {len(si.open_findings)}")
  1615→        print(f"  Existing epics: {len(si.existing_epics)}")
  1616→        print(f"  New since last: {len(si.new_since_last)}")
  1617→        print(f"  Resolved since last: {len(si.resolved_since_last)}")
  1618→        print(colorize("\n  Prompt that would be sent to LLM:", "dim"))
  1619→        print()
  1620→        print(prompt)
  1621→        return
  1622→
  1623→    # Default: dashboard
  1624→    _cmd_synthesize_dashboard(args)
  1625→
  1626→
  1627→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_synthesize"]

709→def _cmd_synthesize_complete(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   710→    """Complete synthesis \u2014 requires organize stage (or confirm-existing path)."""
   711→    strategy: str | None = getattr(args, "strategy", None)
   712→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attestation", None)
   713→    plan = load_plan()
   714→
   715→    if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
   716→        print(colorize("  synthesis::pending is not in the queue \u2014 nothing to complete.", "yellow"))
   717→        return
   718→
   719→    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   720→    stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
   721→
   722→    # Require organize stage confirmed
   723→    if "organize" not in stages:
   724→        if "observe" not in stages:
   725→            print(colorize("  Cannot complete: no stages done yet.", "red"))
   726→            print(colorize('  Start with: desloppify plan synthesize --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
   727→        else:
   728→            print(colorize("  Cannot complete: organize stage not done.", "red"))
   729→            gaps = _unenriched_clusters(plan)
   730→            if gaps:
   731→                print(colorize(f"  {len(gaps)} cluster(s) still need enrichment:", "yellow"))
   732→                for name, missing in gaps:
   733→                    print(colorize(f"    {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}", "yellow"))
   734→                print(colorize(
   735→                    '  Fix: desloppify plan cluster update <name> --description "..." --steps "step1" "step2"',
   736→                    "dim",
   737→                ))
   738→                print(colorize(
   739→                    f"  Then: {SYNTH_CMD_ORGANIZE}",
   740→                    "dim",
   741→                ))
   742→            else:
   743→                manual = _manual_clusters_with_findings(plan)
   744→                if manual:
   745→                    print(colorize("  Clusters are enriched. Record the organize stage first:", "dim"))
   746→                    print(colorize(f"    {SYNTH_CMD_ORGANIZE}", "dim"))
   747→                else:
   748→                    print(colorize("  Create enriched clusters first, then record organize:", "dim"))
   749→                    print(colorize(f"    {SYNTH_CMD_ORGANIZE}", "dim"))
   750→            if meta.get("strategy_summary"):
   751→                print(colorize('  Or fast-track: --confirm-existing --note "why plan is still valid" --strategy "..."', "dim"))
   752→        return
   753→
   754→    # Fold-confirm: auto-confirm organize if attestation provided
   755→    if not stages["organize"].get("confirmed_at"):
   756→        if attestation and len(attestation.strip()) >= _MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN:
   757→            organize_clusters = [n for n in plan.get("clusters", {}) if not plan["clusters"][n].get("auto")]
   758→            validation_err = _validate_attestation(
   759→                attestation.strip(), "organize", cluster_names=organize_clusters,
   760→            )
   761→            if validation_err:
   762→                print(colorize(f"  {validation_err}", "red"))
   763→                return
   764→            organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
   765→            stages["organize"]["confirmed_at"] = utc_now()
   766→            stages["organize"]["confirmed_text"] = attestation.strip()
   767→            save_plan(plan)
   768→            print(colorize("  \u2713 Organize auto-confirmed via --attestation.", "green"))
   769→        else:
   770→            print(colorize("  Cannot complete: organize stage not confirmed.", "red"))

## Summary: Stale Python Bytecode Analysis for Desloppify

Based on my exploration of the codebase, here are my findings on whether stale Python bytecode (.pyc) caches could be an issue for desloppify users:

### 1. **Installation Model: Pip Package (Primary)**
- **pyproject.toml** defines desloppify as a proper pip-installable package with setuptools backend
- **Entry point**: `desloppify` command installed via `[project.scripts]` section pointing to `desloppify.cli:main`
- **Installation methods documented**:
  - `pip install --upgrade "desloppify[full]"` (standard pip install)
  - `pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git` (from GitHub)
  - Editable mode: `pip install -e .` (used in development via Makefile)

### 2. **Invocation Methods**
Three pathways exist:
- **As installed entry point** (standard for pip-installed users): `desloppify scan --path .` → via installed `desloppify` command
- **As module** (for development): `python -m desloppify scan --path .` → via `desloppify/__main__.py` which calls `cli.main()`
- **Direct CLI import** (development): Works via editable install `.pth` file (`.venv/lib/python3.11/site-packages/__editable__.desloppify-0.7.6.pth`)

### 3. **Bytecode Caching: Current State**
- **`.gitignore` properly excludes `__pycache__/`** — developer bytecode never committed to repo
- **Python 3.11.11 bytecode writing is ENABLED** (`sys.flags.dont_write_bytecode: 0`) — no environment-level suppression
- **Substantial `__pycache__` directories exist** during development (confirmed via `find` output showing 10+ directories with .pyc files)
- **No `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` setup** in workflows, Makefile, or code
- **No cache-clearing mechanisms** in `desloppify/cli.py` or `desloppify/__main__.py` — only detector-name LRU cache invalidation, not filesystem bytecode

### 4. **Risk Assessment**

**For pip-installed users (low risk):**
- Pip installations create `.pyc` files in site-packages (e.g., `/usr/local/lib/python3.11/site-packages/desloppify/`)
- Python automatically invalidates stale bytecode via magic number + timestamp checking (PEP 3147)
- When a package is upgraded via `pip install --upgrade`, the old .pyc files are replaced
- Risk is minimal unless users manually edit code in site-packages (rare and unsupported)

**For users running from source (medium risk):**
- Running `python -m desloppify` from a git clone creates `.pyc` files throughout the repo
- After a `git pull` or code update, stale `.pyc` files in `__pycache__/` directories could cause old code to execute
- **Real scenario**: User pulls latest changes, runs desloppify, but `.pyc` files from previous version are still present. Python may use cached bytecode if timestamps align incorrectly
- Python 3.3+ mitigates this with PEP 3147 hash-based invalidation, but edge cases exist

**For development/editable installs (lowest risk):**
- Editable installs use `.pth` files to patch `sys.path` rather than copying code
- Each run dynamically imports from source, reducing bytecode staling issues
- However, Makefile uses `pip install -e .` which still generates `.pyc` files

### 5. **Existing Mitigations**
- `.gitignore` prevents bytecode from entering version control
- Python 3.11's default bytecode invalidation (hash-based in PEP 552 mode)
- Package-smoke test in Makefile (`make package-smoke`) creates a fresh venv and tests the packaged wheel — ensures clean install path works

### 6. **Gaps & Potential Issues**
- **No active cache-clearing in CLI**: Unlike some tools (e.g., `pip cache purge`), desloppify doesn't clean up stale bytecode on first run
- **No documentation warning** to users: No README/docs mention cache-clearing after updates
- **No CI/testing for source-run scenario**: Workflows test pip-installed packages, not source clones after updates
- **No `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` recommendation** for reproducible source-based installs

### 7. **Recommendations for Users**

For users running from source, to avoid bytecode issues after updates:
```bash
# After git pull
find . -type d -name __pycache__ -exec rm -rf {} +
python -m desloppify scan --path .
```

Or set environment variable before running:
```bash
PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python -m desloppify scan --path .
```

### **Conclusion**
The risk of stale bytecode is **primarily theoretical for pip-installed users** (very low) but **practically relevant for source-based users**, especially after code updates. The codebase doesn't currently implement defensive measures, but Python's built-in invalidation mechanisms reduce (though don't eliminate) the risk. A simple enhancement would be to document this in the README or add a startup warning/cache-clear in `cli.py` for development scenarios.

## Summary of Cluster Add Pattern Matching Logic

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's a comprehensive breakdown of how `cluster add` resolves finding IDs from user-provided patterns:

### 1. **Pattern Resolution Flow**

```
User input (patterns) → resolve_ids_from_patterns()
  → match_findings() 
    → _matches_pattern()
  → Plan ID fallback (for synthetic items)
```

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/_resolve.py`

The entry point is `resolve_ids_from_patterns()` which:
- Takes a list of pattern strings and optional plan data
- Calls `match_findings()` from the state module for each pattern
- Falls back to checking plan-only synthetic IDs (like `subjective::*`)
- Returns deduplicated list of matching finding IDs

### 2. **Pattern Matching Logic**

The core logic lives in `_matches_pattern()`:

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` (lines 176-191)

Currently supports these pattern types:

| Pattern Type | Example | Logic |
|---|---|---|
| **Full ID match** | `review::.::name::abc12345` | Exact equality `finding_id == pattern` |
| **Glob patterns** | `review::*::naming` | Uses `fnmatch()` on the finding ID |
| **ID prefix match** | `review::src/file.py::` | `finding_id.startswith(pattern)` when pattern contains `::` |
| **8-char hex suffix** | `f41b3eb7` | Regex match `^[0-9a-f]{8}$` and `finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern)` |
| **Detector name** | `review` | `finding.get("detector") == pattern` |
| **File path match** | `src/foo.py` | Exact match or prefix match with `/` |

### 3. **Short Hash Display & Matching**

The 8-character hex suffix matching is specifically designed for dashboard display:

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/display.py`

```python
def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display."""
    if "::" in fid:
        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
        if len(suffix) >= 8:
            return suffix[:8]
    return fid
```

- Extracts the last segment after `::` from a finding ID
- Returns first 8 characters if suffix is long enough
- Used in CLI output (guardrails, synthesis, import commands)

**Matching logic** (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`, lines 184-185):
```python
_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
    return True
```

### 4. **Dashboard/Show Display**

Finding IDs are displayed with the short hash format in various commands:

- **guardrails.py:** Shows `[short_finding_id(fid)]` with summary when new findings appear
- **review/import_cmd.py:** Lists new items as `[hash] summary`
- **synthesize_handlers.py:** Shows `[hash] [dimension] summary` in unclustered findings
- **render.py (show command):** Displays full ID at bottom: `finding['id']` (line 83)

### 5. **Pattern Hints for Users**

When `cluster add` finds no matches, it displays hints:

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py` (lines 24-31)

```python
def _print_pattern_hints() -> None:
    """Print valid pattern format hints after a no-match error."""
    print(colorize("  Valid patterns:", "dim"))
    print(colorize("    f41b3eb7              (8-char hash suffix from dashboard)", "dim"))
    print(colorize("    review::path::name    (ID prefix)", "dim"))
    print(colorize("    review                (all findings from detector)", "dim"))
    print(colorize("    src/foo.py            (all findings in file)", "dim"))
    print(colorize("    review::*naming*      (glob pattern)", "dim"))
```

### 6. **Related Pattern Matching in Work Queue**

Similar (but slightly different) matching logic exists for filtering work queue items:

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` (lines 63-100)

The `scope_matches()` function is used by `next` and `show` commands. Key differences:
- Also matches against `dimension_name`, `summary`, `kind`
- Uses same fnmatch, prefix, and hex suffix logic
- More permissive for subjective dimensions (contains check on lowercased values)

### 7. **Existing Tests**

**Suffix matching tests:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/plan/test_suffix_matching.py`

Tests cover:
- `test_suffix_match_8char_hex()` - Valid suffix match
- `test_suffix_no_match_*` - Wrong hex, too short, non-hex, uppercase
- `test_match_findings_via_suffix()` - Integration through `match_findings()` API

**Cluster UX tests:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cluster_ux.py`

Tests cover:
- Pattern hints display on no match
- Overlap warning when findings exist in multiple clusters
- Step count feedback on cluster update

### 8. **Key Data Structures**

Finding ID format (from `make_finding()` in filtering.py):
```python
finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
```

Example: `review::.::holistic::dim::name::f41b3eb7`

Breaking down the segments:
1. **Detector:** `review`, `pylint`, etc.
2. **File:** path relative to repo or `.` for codebase-wide
3. **Name/context:** varies by detector (dimension, function name, etc.)
4. **Hash suffix:** 8+ character identifier (optional, mostly used in review findings)

### 9. **Integration Points**

- **cluster add/remove:** Uses `resolve_ids_from_patterns()` directly
- **show command:** Renders findings with full IDs visible at bottom
- **next command:** Uses `scope_matches()` from work queue helpers
- **status/synthesize:** Display short hashes in listings
- **plan operations:** Store full IDs, display short hashes in UI

### 10. **Important Limitations & Behaviors**

1. **Suffix matching requires lowercase hex:** Pattern `F41B3EB7` (uppercase) won't match
2. **Suffix must be exactly 8+ hex chars:** `f41b3eb` (7 chars) won't trigger suffix logic
3. **Glob patterns use fnmatch:** `*` and `?` wildcards, not regex
4. **File path matching is smart:** `src/foo.py` matches exact file or directory prefix
5. **Prefix matching requires `::`:** `review` alone matches by detector, not as prefix
6. **Plan-only IDs included:** Synthetic items (e.g., `subjective::*`) can be matched even if not in state

This architecture provides flexible pattern matching while maintaining backward compatibility and clear user guidance through the pattern hints system.

## Comprehensive Synthesis Attestation Validation & Completion Logic

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here are the key findings:

### 1. **Attestation Validation (_validate_attestation)**

Located in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py` (lines 1272-1306), the validation function checks that attestation text references required data based on stage:

**OBSERVE Stage Validation:**
- **Checks:** Must mention at least one dimension from the open findings
- **Dimension Source:** Extracted from `open_findings[fid]["detail"]["dimension"]` in the current state
- **Matching Logic:** Case-insensitive matching with underscore-to-space replacement (line 1284):
  ```python
  found = [d for d in dimensions if d.lower().replace("_", " ") in text or d.lower() in text]
  ```
  This allows "naming quality" to match "naming_quality" or "naming quality"
- **Helper:** `_observe_dimension_breakdown()` (line 65-73) collects all open finding dimensions and sorts them by frequency

**REFLECT Stage Validation:**
- **Checks:** Must mention at least one dimension OR cluster name
- **Dimension Source:** Uses `detect_recurring_patterns()` to get dimensions with both resolved AND open findings
- **Key Point:** Only dimensions with recurring patterns are checked (lines 1289-1297)
- **Cluster Names:** Only non-auto clusters are validated (line 1408)

**ORGANIZE Stage Validation:**
- **Checks:** Must mention at least one cluster name  
- **Cluster Source:** Non-auto clusters only (line 1301-1304)
- **No Dimensions:** Unlike reflect, organize doesn't validate dimensions

### 2. **Why Review Dimensions Like "naming", "architecture" Could Be Rejected**

The **critical issue** is in how dimension names flow through the system:

1. **Source of Dimension Names:** Review findings come from detector with dimension in `detail.dimension` field
2. **For OBSERVE:** All dimensions from open findings are used - these come from detector output
3. **For REFLECT:** Dimensions come from `detect_recurring_patterns()` which extracts `detail.dimension` from both open and resolved findings
4. **Rejection Reason:** If the review findings don't have properly formatted dimension names (e.g., "naming" vs "naming_quality", or "architecture" vs "cross_module_architecture"), the validation will fail because the dimension name won't match what the user writes

The validator accepts both underscored and spaced versions, but it requires an EXACT dimension name from the actual findings. If the review system outputs "naming" but the subjective dimension is "naming_quality", they won't match.

### 3. **Synthesis Completion & Meta Reset (_apply_completion)**

Located at lines 195-229, completion does the following intentional clearing:

```python
meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
meta.pop("stage_refresh_required", None)
meta.pop("stage_snapshot_hash", None)
```

**What Gets Cleared:**
- `synthesis_stages` dict (observe/reflect/organize stage records) - **intentionally cleared** (line 218)
- `stage_refresh_required` flag
- `stage_snapshot_hash`

**What Gets Preserved/Updated:**
- `finding_snapshot_hash` - updated to current hash (line 208)
- `synthesized_ids` - updated with current open review IDs (line 213)
- `strategy_summary` - preserved unless strategy is "same" (line 214-215)
- `trigger` - set to "manual_synthesis" (line 216)
- `last_completed_at` - updated timestamp (line 217)

**SYNTHESIS_ID Removal:** The `synthesis::pending` item is removed from queue order (lines 199-201)

### 4. **Jump-Back Feedback & Cascade Clear Logic**

Located at lines 50-62 and used throughout stages:

```python
def _cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
    """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*."""
    idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
    cleared: list[str] = []
    for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
        if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
            stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
            stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
            cleared.append(later)
    return cleared
```

**Stage Order:** `["observe", "reflect", "organize"]` (line 47)

**When Triggered:**
- OBSERVE update: Clears reflect + organize confirmations (line 323)
- REFLECT update: Clears organize confirmations (line 454)
- ORGANIZE update: Nothing after it to clear

**Dashboard Messages After Jump-Back:**
- Printed by each stage handler when returning (lines 338-339, 470-471, 667-668)
- Format: `"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}"` in dim color
- Example output: `"  Cleared confirmations on: reflect, organize"`

### 5. **Dashboard Finding Counts**

Multiple locations display findings in clusters:

**In synthesize_handlers.py (_print_progress, line 146-193):**
```python
organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
print(colorize(f"\n  All {organized}/{total} findings are in clusters.", "green"))
```

**In synthesize_handlers.py (_apply_completion, line 225):**
```python
print(colorize(f"  Synthesis complete: {organized}/{total} findings in {cluster_count} cluster(s).", "green"))
```

**In queue_render.py (line 62):**
```python
print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} entries  ({finding_total} findings: {tc_parts}){new_suffix}", "bold"))
```

**_synthesis_coverage Calculation (lines 76-84):**
- Collects all finding IDs from all clusters (across all clusters)
- Counts how many are in the queue_order (excluding SYNTHESIS_ID)
- Returns `(organized, total, clusters)` where:
  - `organized` = count of queue items that are in any cluster
  - `total` = count of all queue items (excluding SYNTHESIS_ID)
  - `clusters` = full cluster dict

**Key Logic:**
```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for synthesis progress."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    queue_ids = [fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []) if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
    organized = sum(1 for fid in queue_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
```

This counts findings that appear in ANY cluster, then measures against all queue items.

## Issues to fix

### 1. Short finding name patterns in `cluster add` (MUST FIX)
**Problem**: Dashboard shows findings as `[timing_a]` (via `short_finding_id()`), but `cluster add my-cluster timing_attack` fails. Only full IDs like `review::src/auth/login_handler.py::timing_attack` work.

**Current matching** in `desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py:176-190` (`_matches_pattern`):
- Full ID match
- Glob with `*`
- Prefix with `::` 
- 8-char hex suffix
- Detector name
- File path

**Missing**: Match by the "name" segment (last `::` segment). E.g., `timing_attack` should match `review::src/auth/login_handler.py::timing_attack`.

**Design**: Add a fallback in `_matches_pattern`: if pattern doesn't contain `::` and doesn't match any other rule, try matching against the last `::` segment of the finding_id. If the last segment starts with the pattern, or the pattern is a substring of the last segment. Be careful: "timing" alone shouldn't match everything with "timing" in any field — scope it to the name segment only.

### 2. Attestation validation accepts wrong dimension names (MUST FIX)
**Problem**: The thorough agent's first two attestation attempts were rejected because they referenced review dimension names ("naming", "architecture") rather than the synthesis-specific recurring dimension names or cluster names.

**Current validation** in `synthesize_handlers.py:1272-1306`:
- Observe: checks against `_observe_dimension_breakdown(si)` dimensions (from `detail.dimension`)
- Reflect: checks against `detect_recurring_patterns()` dimension keys + non-auto cluster names
- Organize: checks against non-auto cluster names

**The issue for REFLECT fold-confirm** (line 574): `reflect_dims` comes from `detect_recurring_patterns()` which only returns dimensions that appear in BOTH open and resolved findings. If a dimension has only open findings (no resolved ones), it won't be in `reflect_dims`, and mentioning it won't count.

**Design**: For reflect validation, also accept the observe-stage dimension names (from `_observe_dimension_breakdown`). This way "naming" works even if there's no recurring pattern for naming. Also improve the error message to list both valid dimension names and cluster names clearly.

### 3. Jump-back feedback is confusing (QUICK FIX)
**Problem**: After jumping back, the dashboard `_print_stage_progress` shows `✓` for all RECORDED stages without distinguishing confirmed vs unconfirmed. Agent sees all checkmarks but can't complete because organize isn't confirmed.

**Current stage progress** (`_print_stage_progress`, line 117): Only checks `stage_name in stages`, not whether stage is confirmed.

**Design**: Show confirmation status in the progress indicator:
- `✓ Stage (confirmed)` — has confirmed_at  
- `✓ Stage (needs confirm)` — recorded but not confirmed
- `→ Stage (current)` — next to do
- `○ Stage` — not started

Also: when cascade-clearing confirmations, the current message "Cleared confirmations on: reflect, organize" is too terse. Add explicit next-step guidance: "Run `--confirm reflect` or pass `--attestation` on the next stage to re-confirm."

### 4. Finding count communicates wrong number (THINK THROUGH)
**Problem**: `_synthesis_coverage()` counts ALL queue_order items (minus SYNTHESIS_ID). This includes non-review items. Shows "4/5 findings in clusters" instead of "24/24 review findings in clusters" because queue also contains deferred/synthetic items.

**Current `_synthesis_coverage`** (line 76-84): counts queue_ids that are in any cluster vs total queue_ids.

**The question**: What SHOULD we communicate? The user cares about:
1. How many review findings are organized — most important during synthesis
2. How many total queue items are organized — relevant for overall plan health
3. Whether any findings are unclustered — the blocker for completion

**Design options**:
- Option A: Change `_synthesis_coverage` to only count review findings (filter by detector == "review" or "concerns")
- Option B: Show both: "24/24 review findings in clusters (25 total queue items)"
- Option C: Use different counts in different contexts — synthesis dashboard counts review findings, overall plan dashboard counts everything

I recommend Option A for the synthesis context. The synthesis workflow is about review findings specifically. In `_synthesis_coverage`, filter to only count items whose IDs match `review::` or `concerns::` prefix. This is consistent with `_apply_completion` which already filters for review/concerns findings.

### 5. Synthesis meta intentionally reset — need archive/warning (DESIGN)
**Problem**: After `--complete`, `epic_synthesis_meta["synthesis_stages"]` is cleared (line 218 in `_apply_completion`). Stage reports (observe/reflect/organize) are gone. Strategy summary IS preserved in `meta["strategy_summary"]`.

**What's preserved**: `strategy_summary`, `synthesized_ids`, `finding_snapshot_hash`, `last_completed_at`, `trigger`
**What's lost**: stage reports (observe/reflect/organize data), their confirmations

**The reset IS intentional** — it allows a new synthesis cycle when new findings arrive. But agents/users lose the detailed analysis.

**Design**: Archive the stage reports before clearing:
```python
if stages:
    meta.setdefault("synthesis_history", []).append({
        "completed_at": utc_now(),
        "stages": dict(stages),  # deep copy
        "strategy": strategy,
    })
meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}
```
Cap the history list at 3 entries (trim oldest). No warning needed — just preserve the data. This way the dashboard can show "Previous synthesis completed at X — run `show synthesis-history` to view" or similar.

Actually, simpler: just archive into `meta["last_synthesis"]` (single previous, not a list). Keep it simple.

### 6. Stale .pyc caches (ASSESS)
**Problem**: Sub-agents running `python -m desloppify` couldn't see new code because of stale .pyc files.

**Analysis**: This ONLY affects source-install/development users. Pip-installed users get fresh .pyc on upgrade. The sub-agent test scenario runs from source.

**Design**: Not a code change — it's a development workflow issue. For sub-agent testing, clear caches before launching agents. Could add a `make clean-pyc` target to Makefile if it doesn't exist. Not worth adding runtime cache-clearing to the CLI itself.

    24→    validate_state_invariants,
    25→)
    26→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
    27→
    28→
    29→def path_scoped_findings(
    30→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→) -> dict[str, Finding]:
    33→    """Filter findings to those within the given scan path."""
    34→    return {
    35→        finding_id: finding
    36→        for finding_id, finding in findings.items()
    37→        if finding_in_scan_scope(str(finding.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    38→    }
    39→
    40→
    41→def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    46→    return (
    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    48→        or file_path == scan_path
    49→        or file_path == "."
    50→    )
    51→
    52→
    53→def open_scope_breakdown(
    54→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    55→    scan_path: str | None,
    56→    *,
    57→    detector: str | None = None,
    58→) -> dict[str, int]:
    59→    """Return open-finding counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    60→    in_scope = 0
    61→    out_of_scope = 0
    62→
    63→    for finding in findings.values():
    64→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    65→            continue
    66→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue
    68→        if detector is not None and finding.get("detector") != detector:
    69→            continue
    70→        file_path = str(finding.get("file", ""))
    71→        if finding_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    72→            in_scope += 1
    73→        else:
    74→            out_of_scope += 1
    75→
    76→    return {
    77→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    78→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    79→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    84→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    85→    return matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    86→
    87→
    88→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    89→    finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    90→) -> str | None:
    91→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    92→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    93→        if "*" in pattern:
    94→            target = finding_id if "::" in pattern else file
    95→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    96→                return pattern
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        if "::" in pattern:
   100→            if finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   101→                return pattern
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        if file == pattern or file == rel(pattern):
   105→            return pattern
   106→
   107→    return None
   108→
   109→
   110→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   111→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   112→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   113→    matched_ids = [
   114→        finding_id
   115→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   116→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   117→    ]
   118→    now = utc_now()
   119→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   120→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   121→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   122→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   123→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   124→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   125→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   126→
   127→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   128→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   129→    return len(matched_ids)
   130→
   131→
   132→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   133→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   134→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   135→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   136→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   137→    if pattern not in ignores:
   138→        ignores.append(pattern)
   139→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
   140→
   141→
   142→def make_finding(
   143→    detector: str,
   144→    file: str,
   145→    name: str,
   146→    *,
   147→    tier: int,
   148→    confidence: str,
   149→    summary: str,
   150→    detail: dict | None = None,
   151→) -> Finding:
   152→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   153→    rfile = rel(file)
   154→    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   155→    now = utc_now()
   156→    return {
   157→        "id": finding_id,
   158→        "detector": detector,
   159→        "file": rfile,
   160→        "tier": tier,
   161→        "confidence": confidence,
   162→        "summary": summary,
   163→        "detail": detail or {},
   164→        "status": "open",
   165→        "note": None,
   166→        "first_seen": now,
   167→        "last_seen": now,
   168→        "resolved_at": None,
   169→        "reopen_count": 0,
   170→    }
   171→
   172→
   173→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
   174→
   175→
   176→def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   177→    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
   178→    if finding_id == pattern:
   179→        return True
   180→    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern):
   181→        return True
   182→    if "::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   183→        return True
   184→    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
   185→        return True
   186→    return (
   187→        finding.get("detector") == pattern
   188→        or finding["file"] == pattern
   189→        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   190→    )
   191→
```

1→"""Shared display utilities for command output."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
     7→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
     8→
     9→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    10→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    11→    """
    12→    if "::" in fid:
    13→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    14→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    15→            return suffix[:8]
    16→    return fid
    17→
    18→
    19→__all__ = ["short_finding_id"]
    20→
