1→"""Pattern → finding-ID resolution for plan commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
     6→from desloppify.state import StateModel, match_findings
     7→
     8→
     9→def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    10→    state: StateModel,
    11→    patterns: list[str],
    12→    *,
    13→    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    14→    status_filter: str = "open",
    15→) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of finding IDs.
    17→
    18→    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    19→    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    20→    have no corresponding entry in ``state["findings"]``.
    21→    """
    22→    seen: set[str] = set()
    23→    result: list[str] = []
    24→
    25→    # Collect all plan IDs for literal-match fallback
    26→    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    27→    if plan is not None:
    28→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    29→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    30→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    31→            plan_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    32→
    33→    for pattern in patterns:
    34→        matches = match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
    35→        if matches:
    36→            for finding in matches:
    37→                fid = finding["id"]
    38→                if fid not in seen:
    39→                    seen.add(fid)
    40→                    result.append(fid)
    41→        elif pattern in plan_ids and pattern not in seen:
    42→            # Literal plan ID (e.g. subjective::foo) not in state findings
    43→            seen.add(pattern)
    44→            result.append(pattern)
    45→    return result
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["resolve_ids_from_patterns"]
    49→
```

    22→    findings = {}

    36→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:

1→"""State resolution operations (match + resolve)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "coerce_assessment_score",
     9→    "match_findings",
    10→    "resolve_findings",
    11→]
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    16→    StateModel,
    17→    ensure_state_defaults,
    18→    utc_now,
    19→    validate_state_invariants,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    22→
    23→
    24→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    25→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    26→
    27→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    28→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    29→    """
    30→    if is_numeric(value):
    31→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    32→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    33→        raw = value.get("score")
    34→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    35→            return None
    36→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    37→    return None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    41→    state: StateModel,
    42→    *,
    43→    status: str,
    44→    resolved_findings: list[dict],
    45→    now: str,
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review findings are resolved.
    48→
    49→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    50→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    51→    for a re-review.
    52→    """
    53→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    54→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    55→        return
    56→
    57→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    58→    for finding in resolved_findings:
    59→        if finding.get("detector") != "review":
    60→            continue
    61→        dimension = str(finding.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    62→        if dimension:
    63→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    64→
    65→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    66→        if dimension not in assessments:
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    70→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    71→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    72→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_finding_{status}"
    73→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    74→        else:
    75→            assessments[dimension] = {
    76→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    77→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    78→                "refresh_reason": f"review_finding_{status}",
    79→                "stale_since": now,
    80→            }
    81→
    82→
    83→def match_findings(
    84→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    85→) -> list[dict]:
    86→    """Return findings matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    87→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    88→    return [
    89→        finding
    90→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
    91→        if not finding.get("suppressed")
    92→        if (status_filter == "all" or finding["status"] == status_filter)
    93→        and _matches_pattern(finding_id, finding, pattern)
    94→    ]
    95→
    96→
    97→def resolve_findings(
    98→    state: StateModel,
    99→    pattern: str,
   100→    status: str,
```

1→"""Tests for jump-back: re-running earlier synthesis stages."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→import desloppify.app.commands.plan.synthesize_handlers as synth_mod
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SYNTHESIS_ID
    10→
    11→
    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Helpers
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→
    16→def _state_with_findings(*ids: str, dimension: str = "naming") -> dict:
    17→    findings = {}
    18→    for fid in ids:
    19→        findings[fid] = {
    20→            "status": "open",
    21→            "detector": "review",
    22→            "file": "test.py",
    23→            "summary": f"Review finding {fid}",
    24→            "confidence": "medium",
    25→            "tier": 2,
    26→            "detail": {"dimension": dimension},
    27→        }
    28→    return {"findings": findings, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    29→
    30→
    31→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    32→    plan = empty_plan()
    33→    plan["queue_order"] = [SYNTHESIS_ID]
    34→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    35→    stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
    36→    for name in stage_names:
    37→        stages[name] = {
    38→            "stage": name,
    39→            "report": (
    40→                "A sufficiently long report for the stage that meets minimum "
    41→                "length requirements and more text to ensure validation passes"
    42→            ),
    43→            "cited_ids": [],
    44→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    45→            "finding_count": 5,
    46→        }
    47→        if confirmed:
    48→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    49→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = (
    50→                "I have thoroughly reviewed all the findings in naming dimension "
    51→                "and this stage analysis is complete"
    52→            )
    53→    return plan
    54→
    55→
    56→def _plan_with_enriched_clusters(stage_names, confirmed=False):
    57→    """Plan with stages + enriched manual clusters."""
    58→    plan = _plan_with_stages(*stage_names, confirmed=confirmed)
    59→    plan["clusters"]["fix-naming"] = {
    60→        "name": "fix-naming",
    61→        "finding_ids": ["r1", "r2"],
    62→        "description": "Fix naming issues",
    63→        "action_steps": ["step 1", "step 2"],
    64→        "auto": False,
    65→        "user_modified": True,
    66→        "created_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    67→        "updated_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    68→        "cluster_key": "",
    69→        "action": None,
    70→    }
    71→    return plan
    72→
    73→
    74→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    75→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    76→
    77→
    78→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    79→    defaults = {
    80→        "lang": None,
    81→        "path": ".",
    82→        "confirm": None,
    83→        "attestation": None,
    84→        "confirmed": None,
    85→        "stage": None,
    86→        "report": None,
    87→        "complete": False,
    88→        "confirm_existing": False,
    89→        "strategy": None,
    90→        "note": None,
    91→        "start": False,
    92→        "dry_run": False,
    93→    }
    94→    defaults.update(overrides)
    95→    return argparse.Namespace(**defaults)

    99→# Tests: jump-back reflect with new report

   101→
   102→class TestJumpBackReflect:
   103→    def test_rerun_reflect_with_new_report_clears_organize_confirmation(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
   104→        """Re-running reflect with --report clears organize's confirmed_at."""
   105→        plan = _plan_with_enriched_clusters(
   106→            ["observe", "reflect", "organize"], confirmed=True,
   107→        )
   108→        state = _state_with_findings("r1", "r2", "r3", "r4", "r5")
   109→
   110→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
   111→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
   112→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
   113→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "save_plan", lambda p: None)
   114→
   115→        new_report = (
   116→            "Revised strategy: after further analysis, the naming issues "
   117→            "have a deeper root cause than initially thought. Need to "
   118→            "restructure approach to address the root cause first."
   119→        )
   120→        args = _fake_args(stage="reflect", report=new_report)
   121→        synth_mod.cmd_plan_synthesize(args)
   122→
   123→        stages = plan["epic_synthesis_meta"]["synthesis_stages"]
   124→        # Reflect should have new report
   125→        assert stages["reflect"]["report"] == new_report
   126→        # Reflect's own confirmation should be cleared (new data)
   127→        assert stages["reflect"].get("confirmed_at") is None
   128→        # Organize's confirmation should be cleared (cascade)
   129→        assert stages["organize"].get("confirmed_at") is None
   130→

   132→        """Re-running reflect without --report reuses existing report."""

   139→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)

   145→        stages = plan["epic_synthesis_meta"]["synthesis_stages"]

   150→

   175→        """Jumping back to observe with new report clears reflect + organize confirmations."""

   206→class TestJumpBackThenFoldConfirm:
   207→    def test_jump_back_then_fold_confirm_forward(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
   208→        """Jump back to reflect (reuse), then --stage organize with --attestation fold-confirms."""
   209→        plan = _plan_with_enriched_clusters(

   216→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
   217→        monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "save_plan", lambda p: None)

   220→        args = _fake_args(stage="reflect")

   226→        assert stages["organize"].get("confirmed_at") is None
   227→
   228→        # Step 2: Re-run organize with attestation to fold-confirm reflect
   229→        # Since reflect is still confirmed from reuse, this should work directly
   230→        organize_report = (
   231→            "Re-organized after reviewing. The fix-naming cluster structure "

   245→
   246→        stages = plan["epic_synthesis_meta"]["synthesis_stages"]
   247→        assert "organize" in stages

    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Helpers
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→
    16→def _state_with_findings(*ids: str, dimension: str = "naming") -> dict:
    17→    findings = {}
    18→    for fid in ids:
    19→        findings[fid] = {
    20→            "status": "open",
    21→            "detector": "review",
    22→            "file": "test.py",
    23→            "summary": f"Review finding {fid}",
    24→            "confidence": "medium",
    25→            "tier": 2,
    26→            "detail": {"dimension": dimension},
    27→        }
    28→    return {"findings": findings, "scan_count": 5, "dimension_scores": {}}
    29→
    30→
    31→def _plan_with_stages(*stage_names: str, confirmed: bool = False) -> dict:
    32→    plan = empty_plan()
    33→    plan["queue_order"] = [SYNTHESIS_ID]
    34→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    35→    stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
    36→    for name in stage_names:
    37→        stages[name] = {
    38→            "stage": name,
    39→            "report": (
    40→                "A sufficiently long report for the stage that meets minimum "
    41→                "length requirements and more text to ensure validation passes"
    42→            ),
    43→            "cited_ids": [],
    44→            "timestamp": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    45→            "finding_count": 5,
    46→        }
    47→        if confirmed:
    48→            stages[name]["confirmed_at"] = "2025-06-01T00:01:00Z"
    49→            stages[name]["confirmed_text"] = (
    50→                "I have thoroughly reviewed all the findings in naming dimension "
    51→                "and this stage analysis is complete"
    52→            )
    53→    return plan
    54→
    55→
    56→def _plan_with_enriched_clusters(stage_names, confirmed=False):
    57→    """Plan with stages + enriched manual clusters (for organize tests)."""
    58→    plan = _plan_with_stages(*stage_names, confirmed=confirmed)
    59→    plan["clusters"]["fix-naming"] = {
    60→        "name": "fix-naming",
    61→        "finding_ids": ["r1", "r2"],
    62→        "description": "Fix naming issues",
    63→        "action_steps": ["step 1", "step 2"],
    64→        "auto": False,
    65→        "user_modified": True,
    66→        "created_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    67→        "updated_at": "2025-06-01T00:00:00Z",
    68→        "cluster_key": "",
    69→        "action": None,
    70→    }
    71→    return plan
    72→
    73→
    74→def _fake_runtime(state: dict):
    75→    return type("Ctx", (), {"state": state, "config": {}})()
    76→
    77→
    78→def _fake_args(**overrides) -> argparse.Namespace:
    79→    defaults = {
    80→        "lang": None,
    81→        "path": ".",
    82→        "confirm": None,
    83→        "attestation": None,
    84→        "confirmed": None,
    85→        "stage": None,
    86→        "report": None,
    87→        "complete": False,
    88→        "confirm_existing": False,
    89→        "strategy": None,
    90→        "note": None,
    91→        "start": False,
    92→        "dry_run": False,
    93→    }
    94→    defaults.update(overrides)
    95→    return argparse.Namespace(**defaults)

## Detailed Implementation Plan: Round 3 -- Agent UX Friction Fixes

### Overview

Six issues from sub-agent testing, ordered by priority and dependency. The changes touch primarily two files (`filtering.py` and `synthesize_handlers.py`) plus test files. I will present each issue with the exact code locations, the change needed, edge cases to handle, and the test strategy.

---

### Issue 1: Short finding name patterns in `cluster add` (MUST FIX)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`

**Current code** (lines 176-190):

```python
def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
    if finding_id == pattern:
        return True
    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern):
        return True
    if "::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern):
        return True
    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
        return True
    return (
        finding.get("detector") == pattern
        or finding["file"] == pattern
        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
    )
```

**Problem**: When `short_finding_id()` returns `timing_a` (the first 8 chars of `timing_attack`), the user or agent might type `timing_attack` as a pattern. This does not match any existing rule because:
- It is not a full ID
- No `*` so no glob
- No `::` so no prefix
- `_HEX8_RE` won't match (it requires hex chars only)
- It is not a detector name, file path, or directory prefix

**Change**: Add a name-segment match as the final fallback, AFTER the existing detector/file checks. The name segment is the last `::` part of the finding ID.

```python
def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, name, or path."""
    if finding_id == pattern:
        return True
    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern):
        return True
    if "::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern):
        return True
    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
        return True
    if (
        finding.get("detector") == pattern
        or finding["file"] == pattern
        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
    ):
        return True
    # Name-segment match: pattern without :: matches the last segment of the ID
    if "::" not in pattern and "::" in finding_id:
        name_segment = finding_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
        if name_segment == pattern:
            return True
    return False
```

**Design decisions**:
- Use **exact match** on the name segment only (`name_segment == pattern`), NOT substring or prefix. Substring matching (`timing` matching `timing_attack`) would be too broad and could cause surprising results. The issue description suggests `startswith` or `substring`, but I recommend exact match because (a) `short_finding_id` shows the FULL last segment when it is not an 8-char hex suffix (e.g., `timing_attack`), and (b) partial substring matching on a non-delimited pattern is dangerous for commands that mutate state like `cluster add`.
- The guard `"::" not in pattern` prevents this from interfering with the prefix match rule.
- This fires after detector/file checks, so detector names like "review" still work via the existing rule.

**Also update** `_print_pattern_hints()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py` (line 24-31) to add:
```
    print(colorize("    timing_attack         (finding name — last :: segment)", "dim"))
```

**Test file**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/plan/test_suffix_matching.py` -- add new tests to the existing file:

```python
def test_name_segment_exact_match():
    """Name segment (last :: part) matches when pattern equals it exactly."""
    fid = "review::src/auth/login.py::timing_attack"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "src/auth/login.py"}
    assert _matches_pattern(fid, finding, "timing_attack") is True

def test_name_segment_no_partial_match():
    """Partial name segment does not match (prevent overbroad matching)."""
    fid = "review::src/auth/login.py::timing_attack"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "src/auth/login.py"}
    assert _matches_pattern(fid, finding, "timing") is False

def test_name_segment_no_match_with_colons():
    """Pattern containing :: is NOT treated as name-segment match."""
    fid = "review::src/auth/login.py::timing_attack"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "src/auth/login.py"}
    assert _matches_pattern(fid, finding, "review::timing_attack") is False
    # BUT it matches via prefix rule:
    # "review::src/auth/login.py::timing_attack".startswith("review::timing_attack") => False
    # So this should be False overall. Good.

def test_name_segment_hash_suffix_still_works():
    """8-char hex suffix matching still takes priority over name segment."""
    fid = "review::.::holistic::dim::name::f41b3eb7"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "test.py"}
    assert _matches_pattern(fid, finding, "f41b3eb7") is True

def test_name_segment_does_not_shadow_detector():
    """Detector match should still work for patterns that are detector names."""
    fid = "review::test.py::some_name"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "test.py"}
    assert _matches_pattern(fid, finding, "review") is True  # via detector rule
```

### Issue 2: Attestation validation accepts wrong dimension names (MUST FIX)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`

**Problem location 1** -- `_cmd_stage_organize` fold-confirm (lines 569-582):

```python
if not stages["reflect"].get("confirmed_at"):
    if attestation and len(attestation.strip()) >= _MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN:
        runtime = command_runtime(args)
        si = collect_synthesis_input(plan, runtime.state)
        recurring = detect_recurring_patterns(si.open_findings, si.resolved_findings)
        reflect_dims = list(recurring.keys()) if recurring else []
        reflect_clusters = [n for n in plan.get("clusters", {}) if not plan["clusters"][n].get("auto")]
        validation_err = _validate_attestation(
            attestation.strip(), "reflect",
            dimensions=reflect_dims, cluster_names=reflect_clusters,
        )
```

The issue: `reflect_dims` only contains dimensions from `detect_recurring_patterns()`, which requires BOTH open AND resolved findings in the same dimension. On a first synthesis (no resolved findings), `reflect_dims` is empty. If there are also no manual clusters yet, the `dimensions` and `cluster_names` are both empty lists, so `_validate_attestation` passes trivially (no data to check against). But if there ARE resolved findings in some dimensions but not others, mentioning an observe-stage dimension like "naming" or "architecture" will fail validation because those aren't in `recurring_dims`.

**Change**: In ALL three locations where reflect dimensions are assembled for validation, also include the observe-stage dimensions:

1. **Line 574** (`_cmd_stage_organize` fold-confirm): Add observe dimensions.
2. **Line 1407** (`_confirm_reflect`): Add observe dimensions.
3. **Line 393** (`_cmd_stage_reflect` fold-confirm for observe): This is for observe validation, which already uses `_observe_dimension_breakdown` correctly -- no change needed.

The change at each location:

```python
# Existing:
reflect_dims = list(recurring.keys()) if recurring else []

# New -- also accept observe-stage dimensions:
_by_dim, observe_dim_names = _observe_dimension_breakdown(si)
reflect_dims = sorted(set(
    (list(recurring.keys()) if recurring else []) + observe_dim_names
))
```

This way an attestation mentioning "naming" will pass even if "naming" only has open findings (no recurring pattern).

**Also improve error messages** in `_validate_attestation` (lines 1289-1297) for the reflect case:

```python
elif stage == "reflect":
    refs: list[str] = []
    if dimensions:
        refs.extend(d for d in dimensions if d.lower().replace("_", " ") in text or d.lower() in text)
    if cluster_names:
        refs.extend(n for n in cluster_names if n.lower() in text)
    if not refs and (dimensions or cluster_names):
        dim_hint = ", ".join(dimensions[:4]) if dimensions else "(none)"
        cluster_hint = ", ".join(cluster_names[:4]) if cluster_names else "(none)"
        return (
            f"Attestation must reference at least one dimension or cluster name.\n"
            f"  Valid dimensions: {dim_hint}\n"
            f"  Valid clusters: {cluster_hint}"
        )
```

**Test additions** in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_synthesis_attestation.py`:

```python
def test_validate_reflect_accepts_observe_dimension(self):
    """Text mentioning an observe dimension (not recurring) passes for reflect."""
    err = _validate_attestation(
        "My strategy addresses the naming dimension patterns and all issues found in the codebase",
        "reflect",
        dimensions=["naming", "coupling"],  # includes observe dims
        cluster_names=["fix-naming"],
    )
    assert err is None

def test_validate_reflect_shows_both_dims_and_clusters_in_error(self):
    """Error message for reflect shows both dimension and cluster names."""
    err = _validate_attestation(
        "I have reviewed everything thoroughly and the plan covers all aspects",
        "reflect",
        dimensions=["naming", "coupling"],
        cluster_names=["fix-naming"],
    )
    assert err is not None
    assert "naming" in err
    assert "fix-naming" in err
```

And an integration test through the fold-confirm path showing that observe-dimension names work in `_cmd_stage_organize` fold-confirm for reflect.

### Issue 3: Jump-back feedback is confusing (QUICK FIX)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`

**Function**: `_print_stage_progress` (lines 117-143)

**Current code**:

```python
def _print_stage_progress(stages: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
    print(colorize("  Stages:", "dim"))
    for stage_name, label in SYNTHESIS_STAGE_LABELS:
        if stage_name in stages:
            print(colorize(f"    ✓ {label}", "green"))
        elif SYNTHESIS_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES[stage_name].issubset(stages):
            print(colorize(f"    → {label} (current)", "yellow"))
        else:
            print(colorize(f"    ○ {label}", "dim"))
```

**Change**: Distinguish confirmed vs needs-confirm states:

```python
def _print_stage_progress(stages: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
    print(colorize("  Stages:", "dim"))
    for stage_name, label in SYNTHESIS_STAGE_LABELS:
        if stage_name in stages:
            if stages[stage_name].get("confirmed_at"):
                print(colorize(f"    ✓ {label} (confirmed)", "green"))
            else:
                print(colorize(f"    ✓ {label} (needs confirm)", "yellow"))
        elif SYNTHESIS_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES[stage_name].issubset(stages):
            print(colorize(f"    → {label} (current)", "yellow"))
        else:
            print(colorize(f"    ○ {label}", "dim"))
```

Note: "commit" is never stored in stages (it is the `--complete` action), so we only check confirm status for observe/reflect/organize.

**Also enhance cascade-clear feedback** in the three places where `cleared` is printed (lines 297, 339, 471, 668):

Current:
```python
if cleared:
    print(colorize(f"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}", "dim"))
```

Change to:
```python
if cleared:
    print(colorize(f"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}", "yellow"))
    next_unconfirmed = next((s for s in _STAGE_ORDER if s in stages and not stages[s].get("confirmed_at")), None)
    if next_unconfirmed:
        print(colorize(
            f"  Re-confirm with: desloppify plan synthesize --confirm {next_unconfirmed} --attestation \"...\"",
            "dim",
        ))
```

**Test**: Add a test to `test_synthesis_jump_back.py` verifying that after a cascade-clear, the output contains "needs confirm" and re-confirm guidance.

### Issue 4: Finding count communicates wrong number (THINK THROUGH)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`

**Function**: `_synthesis_coverage` (lines 76-84)

**Current code**:

```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for synthesis progress."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    queue_ids = [fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []) if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
    organized = sum(1 for fid in queue_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
```

**Problem**: `queue_ids` includes ALL queue items -- mechanical findings, deferred items, subjective items -- not just review findings. This shows "4/5 findings in clusters" instead of "24/24 review findings in clusters".

**The approach**: Option A from the issue -- filter to review/concerns findings only. The synthesis workflow is specifically about review findings. This is consistent with `_apply_completion` (lines 209-212) which already filters for `detector in ("review", "concerns")`.

However, `_synthesis_coverage` does not have access to the state/findings to check the detector. The queue_order only contains finding IDs (strings). We need to either:

(a) Filter by ID prefix (`review::` or `concerns::`), or
(b) Accept the state as an additional parameter.

Option (a) is simpler and sufficient since finding IDs always start with `{detector}::` per `make_finding()` in filtering.py (line 154). Both `review::` and `concerns::` are the review detectors.

**Change**:

```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review findings in synthesis."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    # Only count review/concerns findings — synthesis is about these
    review_ids = [
        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
        if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]
    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
```

**Callers to verify**: `_synthesis_coverage` is called at:
- Line 191: `_print_progress` -- "All X/Y findings are in clusters" (synthesis context)
- Line 197: `_apply_completion` -- coverage stats at completion
- Line 764: `_cmd_synthesize_complete` fold-confirm for organize
- Line 800: `_cmd_synthesize_complete` verification
- Line 854: `_cmd_synthesize_complete` summary
- Line 1260: `_show_plan_summary` -- coverage display
- Line 1491: `_confirm_organize`

All callers are in synthesis context, so filtering to review findings is correct for all of them.

**Test**: Create a focused test showing that non-review items in queue_order are excluded from the count:

```python
def test_synthesis_coverage_excludes_non_review():
    plan = empty_plan()
    plan["queue_order"] = [
        SYNTHESIS_ID,
        "review::test.py::naming::abc12345",
        "review::test.py::coupling::def67890",
        "complexity::test.py::high_complexity",  # non-review
    ]
    plan["clusters"]["fix-naming"] = {
        "finding_ids": ["review::test.py::naming::abc12345"],
    }
    organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    assert total == 2  # only review findings
    assert organized == 1
```

### Issue 5: Synthesis meta intentionally reset -- need archive (DESIGN)

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`

**Function**: `_apply_completion` (lines 195-229)

**Current code at line 218**:

```python
meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
```

**Change**: Archive the stage reports into `meta["last_synthesis"]` before clearing:

```python
stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
if stages:
    meta["last_synthesis"] = {
        "completed_at": utc_now(),
        "stages": {k: dict(v) for k, v in stages.items()},
        "strategy": strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", ""),
    }
meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
```

This preserves a single previous synthesis cycle. Each new completion overwrites the previous archive. This is simple and sufficient -- we don't need a full history list.

**No additional UI changes needed** at this point. The data is preserved for potential future `show synthesis-history` commands or for agents that want to reference previous analysis.

**Test**: In the existing `test_synthesis_fold_confirm.py` class `TestCompleteFoldConfirmOrganize`, add an assertion that after completion, `meta["last_synthesis"]` contains the stage data:

```python
def test_complete_archives_stages(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    """Completion archives stage reports into last_synthesis."""
    plan = _plan_with_enriched_clusters(
        ["observe", "reflect", "organize"], confirmed=True,
    )
    state = _state_with_findings("r1", "r2", "r3", "r4", "r5")
    
    monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
    monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
    monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
    monkeypatch.setattr(synth_mod, "save_plan", lambda p: None)
    
    strategy = "..." # 200+ chars
    args = _fake_args(complete=True, strategy=strategy)
    synth_mod.cmd_plan_synthesize(args)
    
    meta = plan["epic_synthesis_meta"]
    assert meta["synthesis_stages"] == {}  # cleared
    assert "last_synthesis" in meta
    assert "observe" in meta["last_synthesis"]["stages"]
    assert "reflect" in meta["last_synthesis"]["stages"]
    assert "organize" in meta["last_synthesis"]["stages"]
    assert meta["last_synthesis"]["completed_at"]
```

### Issue 6: Stale .pyc caches (ASSESS -- NO CODE CHANGE)

This is a development workflow issue, not a product issue. No code changes needed. For sub-agent testing, run `find . -name '*.pyc' -delete && find . -name '__pycache__' -type d -exec rm -rf {} +` before launching agents, or set `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in the agent environment.

---

### Implementation Order

The changes should be implemented in this sequence:

**Step 1**: Issue 1 (name-segment matching in `_matches_pattern`). This is the simplest, most isolated change. It only touches `filtering.py` and the test file. It unblocks agent usage of `cluster add` with readable names.

**Step 2**: Issue 4 (finding count). Small change to `_synthesis_coverage` in `synthesize_handlers.py`. Should be done before Issues 2/3/5 to avoid merge conflicts within the same file.

**Step 3**: Issue 3 (stage progress display). Changes `_print_stage_progress` and the cascade-clear messages. Purely output changes, low risk.

**Step 4**: Issue 2 (attestation validation). Requires changes in three locations within `synthesize_handlers.py` plus the `_validate_attestation` error messages. More involved but well-scoped.

**Step 5**: Issue 5 (archive stages). Small addition to `_apply_completion`. Should go last because it depends on understanding the completion flow, and the test should run after other changes are stable.

**Total lines of change estimate**:
- `filtering.py`: ~6 lines added
- `synthesize_handlers.py`: ~30 lines changed/added across 5 locations
- `cluster_handlers.py`: 1 line added (hint text)
- Test files: ~80-100 lines of new tests across 3-4 files

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` - Core `_matches_pattern` function to add name-segment matching (Issue 1)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py` - Attestation validation, stage progress display, coverage counting, and archive logic (Issues 2, 3, 4, 5)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/plan/test_suffix_matching.py` - Existing pattern-matching tests to extend for name-segment matching

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py` - Pattern hints to update with name-segment example
